# RBAC Wave 0b — new permissions for the remaining ungated writes Implementation Plan

**Revision: rev 2** (2026-09-10) — fix round on Codex plan gate r1. Register: `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-plan-0b1-codex-gate-r1.md`; prompt: `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-plan-gate-RBAC-0b1-round-1-2026-09-10.md`. The ID-by-ID mapping is the **Change log rev 1 → rev 2** section at the end of this document, together with a **Signature changes for wave 1** section (there are none; two additions wave 1 should consume are named).

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Declare the nineteen permission keys that do not exist yet, gate the twenty-five live write routes and sixteen read routes that need them, and ship the three pieces of machinery those keys cannot reach a live tenant without — `PermissionWriteLock` (adopting `lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s advisory key and row order), `TenantFleetRunner` (the one fleet iterator) and `permissions:ensure` / `permissions:ensure-fleet`. Plus the five items wave 0a had to defer for file-overlap reasons, the **four Identity read-route gates and their page guards** relocated here by plan gate r1 (task **0b-15**), and the CI `env:` line that switches on wave 0a's anti-growth direction.

**Architecture:** Wave 0b still writes the catalogue through `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`'s two arrays — the registry is wave 1 — but it stops *deploying* through `tenants:seed`, because after `lane/w-lot-a-1a` merges that seeder writes **nothing at all** on a marked tenant with `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE=false` (its shipped default). The wave therefore ships a small additive command, `permissions:ensure`, whose per-tenant routine runs on the shared `TenantFleetRunner`, whose grants are equality-guarded by `LegacyRoleBaseline::matchesPreWave0b()`, and whose whole run sits inside one transaction holding the **same** per-tenant advisory lock the incoming lane's `LotActionPermissionDelta` already takes. Every other runtime role-grant writer is wired into that lock in the same commit, because an equality check that is not serialized against the operator edit path is not a guard.

**Tech Stack:** Laravel 12 / PHP 8.4 / Spatie laravel-permission 6.25 / Stancl Tenancy 3.10 / PHPUnit 11 / PHPStan L8 / Pint / React 19 / Vitest

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` (rev 9, **ACCEPTED** — Codex spec gate r9, 0 BLOCKER / 0 MAJOR / 3 minor editorial, register at `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-spec-codex-gate-r9.md`). Wave 0b's authoritative rows are §8 "Wave 0b", §4.3.5, §4.3.2's writer inventory, §4.3.3's `TenantFleetRunner` contract, §4.4.4, §4.9.3, §4.9.4 and §6.

**Master plan:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-programme-execution-plan.md` — this plan does not contradict it; where it adds something the master plan does not carry, it is named in **Decisions and open questions** below.

**Sibling plan:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-0a.md` (**rev 3**). Residual **R-3** of that plan is discharged here, by Task 12. *(Its **R-4** — CI registration — is **closed in wave 0a itself** as of 0a rev 2, so Task 12 is narrowed to the `env:` line. Note the corrected reason, 0a rev 3: R-3 waits on an **owner-held repository variable**, not on file access — wave 0a edits `.github/workflows/ci.yml` itself.)*

## Global Constraints

- [ ] **Entry condition is a merge, not a hope.** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` must be an ancestor of local `dev` before Task 1 starts. The exact check is in Phase 0 and is `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, not a `branch --merged` grep — a grep matches a substring and a deleted-then-recreated branch name.
- [ ] **Middleware set (CLAUDE.md rule 12):** every module `routes.php` group keeps `['api', 'auth:sanctum', SetPermissionsTeam::class, EnforceTokenTenantClaim::class]` plus whatever `module:<Name>` gate it already carries. This wave **adds** `can:` to existing routes; it never rewrites, reorders or removes an existing middleware.
- [ ] **`can:` only.** The action gate is `can:` (or `require.any.permission:` for an any-of). Never `permission:` — that alias has never existed in this repository. Never a controller check or a FormRequest `authorize()` as the *only* gate (spec §4.4.1 E-2/E-4).
- [ ] **A flag-conditional middleware is not a gate.** `BatchActionAccess` returns `$next($request)` with no check at all while `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE` is false (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/Middleware/BatchActionAccess.php:18-20`, default `false` at `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/config/lot_action_permissions.php:5`). 0b-5 adds a real `can:` **beside** it, never instead of it.
- [ ] **Nineteen keys, and not a twentieth.** The key list is fixed by §8 and by `LegacyRoleBaseline`'s v0 arithmetic (§4.3.0a rule 1: `admin` gets **all 19**, `manager` and `general_manager` get **seven**, every other 0b key reaches no template but `admin`). Adding a key means editing `wave0bAdditions`, the deploy `--keys` list and `Wave0bAdditionConstantTest` in the same commit — see **Q-0b-2**.
- [ ] **`WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS['admin']` is all nineteen, and that is NOT the same thing as `--grant-to=admin`** (plan gate r1 **B0b-1**). The constant is wave 1's **adoption oracle input**: `version0('admin') = postA1aGrants('admin') ∪ wave0bAdditions('admin')`, and after this wave's deploy a pristine `admin` holds all nineteen — because `EnsurePermissionsRunner` grants `admin` **unconditionally**, on every invocation, for every key in `--keys`. Declaring `admin`'s additions as `[]` (rev 1's shape) would make an ensure-provisioned *pristine* `admin` compare **unequal** to v0 during wave-1 adoption and be classified `adopted_customised` — the exact opposite of the eight-pristine-role state §4.3.0a requires and wave 1's supersession test expects. The **deployable** subset is separate and smaller: only the **seven** `manager`/`general_manager` keys are ever passed as `--grant-to`, because `admin` needs no `--grant-to` to receive anything. Two properties, two assertions, one constant — see Task 1 Step 4 and Task 6 Step 4.
- [ ] **No commit in this wave is knowingly red** (plan gate r1 **B0b-2**). Rev 1 staged a test in Task 1 whose catalogue assertion could not pass until Task 6, and simply omitted the file from Task 1's run list. That is not an independently mergeable commit and it destroys the red/green evidence model, because a failure at Task 3 could no longer be distinguished from the failure the plan already expected. **Every task's commit must be green in isolation**, and every task below states its **run list per commit** — the exact command whose green output is that commit's evidence. A test whose subject does not exist yet ships in the commit that creates its subject.
- [ ] **The lock key is `lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s, adopted verbatim: `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId`, hashed with `hashtextextended(?, 0)`** (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:131-136`). Row order is **`roles.name` ASC, then `roles.id` ASC** (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:…/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:111-116`). Two advisory namespaces are two serialization domains, i.e. no serialization at all; two row orders are a deadlock. Never invent a second key or a second order.
- [ ] **NULL-team tolerance everywhere.** Every role lookup in this wave resolves `guard_name = 'sanctum' AND name IN (…) AND (tenant_id IS NULL OR tenant_id = :tenantId)`, preserves `tenant_id` **as found**, orders by `name` then `id`, and takes `lockForUpdate()`. Two matching rows for one name is `ambiguous_legacy_role_collision`; an unmarked `general_manager` is `unmarked_general_manager_collision`. This is `LotActionPermissionDelta::resolveCatalogueRoles()` verbatim (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:…/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:111-129`). **No role is ever re-homed.**
- [ ] **Constructor injection only** (`private readonly`), never the `app()` helper in production code. **Strict types** (`declare(strict_types=1);` in every new PHP file), no `mixed`, no `any`. Enums for every status/type value this wave introduces.
- [ ] **PHPUnit by path only.** The full backend suite is never run locally (laptop rule). Every command in this plan names a file or a directory.
- [ ] **PG lane env — this wave has real PostgreSQL legs, and they run under the repository's own PG harness** (plan gate r1 **M0b-5**). Six of its tests are PG-only by construction (two-connection races, two real tenant databases, advisory locks). `apps/api/phpunit-pgsql.xml:2-16` is the configuration that forces `DB_CONNECTION=pgsql` and documents the invocation; rev 1's commands exported the database variables but then ran the **default** `phpunit.xml`, which is not the harness CI uses. Use a **private per-session** database, override **both** database variables, and **always pass `-c phpunit-pgsql.xml`**:
      ```
      DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_b0 DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_b0_central DB_CONNECTION=pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 ./vendor/bin/phpunit -c phpunit-pgsql.xml <path>
      ```
      This exact prefix is written out at every PG command site below; where a step refers back to it, it is called **the PG harness prefix**. Never the shared default database; never two PG legs in parallel on this laptop.
- [ ] **Commit subjects follow `AGENTS.md:15-16`** (added in rev 1.1, from the wave-0a plan's gate r1 M-6): *"Commits must follow `Phase <major.minor.patch>: <imperative summary>`"*. **Wave 0b uses `Phase 0.2.<task number>`** — wave 0a uses `Phase 0.1.<task>`. Task 12 and task 13b (0b-15) below already carry that form; **the other tasks' subjects were written before this rule was stated and still show the `feat(...)` / `test(...)` shape — convert each to `Phase 0.2.<task>: <imperative summary>` when its commit is actually written, keeping the body verbatim.** Orchestrator documents (this plan, gate registers, the handback) keep the house `docs(...)` / `review(...)` convention; `AGENTS.md`'s rule governs the code history.
- [ ] **Path-scoped commits.** `git add <explicit paths>` only — never `git add -A`, never `git commit -a`. Every commit ends with the two trailers:
      ```
      Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
      Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Bp1WuD39YqaMWiHR8vPDNN
      ```
- [ ] **Worktree:** `git worktree add .worktrees/rbac-w0b -b lane/rbac-w0b dev` — run from the shared checkout, then work only inside `.worktrees/rbac-w0b`. Never edit or commit in the shared `dev` worktree (CLAUDE.md rule 21).
- [ ] **No `origin` push.** `origin/dev` auto-deploys staging including `tenants:migrate`. This wave merges into **local** `dev` and is promoted by the owner in a verified fast-forward batch.
- [ ] **Lane cap is 3.** While 0b runs, `lane/t2-receipt-spine` may still be open; that is two lanes. Do not open a third RBAC lane.
- [ ] **Empirical evidence, not compilation** (owner rule, 2026-08-31). Task 14 names two UI probes with 5xx and browser-console capture. "Tests pass" is never the evidence for a wave that changes what a live page can call — and this wave changes twenty-five of them.

### Convention 09 (second-of-everything)

`roles`, `permissions` and `users` are already carried in the exclusion map of `TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest` with stated reasons (`apps/api/tests/Architecture/TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest.php:131-132,160,175`); this wave adds no table, no column and no unique key, so the obligation is **not to invalidate an existing exclusion**. But two of the three axes are live behaviour and are real tests here (spec §6, wave 0b row):

| Axis | Test | What it asserts, on **rows**, never on status alone |
|---|---|---|
| **Second company** | `ServicePermissionsSecondCompanyTest` | an actor holding `services.update` in company A is refused `PATCH /services/{id}` on a service of company B — the new gate did not displace the existing company scope. |
| **Second company** | `CategoryPermissionsSecondCompanyTest` | categories are **company**-scoped with no location binding at all (`apps/api/app/Modules/Product/Presentation/Controllers/CategoryController.php:28-35,101-108,115-178`), so the axis categories actually have is the company one. Asserts the rows returned and written, per company. |
| **Second location** | `StockAdjustmentLocationScopePermissionTest` | a principal **holding** `inventory.adjustments.create` but restricted by `allowed_location_ids` to location A is refused the adjustment at location B (`apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/Requests/StoreStockAdjustmentRequest.php:90`, `new ValidLocationAccess(...)`), and removing the restriction lets the identical payload through. Asserts the `stock_adjustments` rows. |
| **Re-run / idempotency** | `EnsurePermissionsIdempotencyTest` | `permissions:ensure` run twice reports `created=0 granted=0` on the second run **and writes zero rows**, asserted by query-log count. |

**The batch gap is not silently dropped.** 0b-5 gates `DELETE /batches/{uuid}` and `POST /batches/{uuid}/recall` on **permission only**; neither consults `LocationScopeResolver` (`apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/Controllers/BatchController.php:176-188,193-210` — the resolver's only use in that controller is the expiry read at `:252`), and the lane's `BatchActionAccess` checks activation only. That is filed as `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-batch-writes-ignore-location-scope.md` for wave 3 and is named in the handback as residual **R-0b-3**.

## Decisions and open questions

Four places where the accepted spec does not determine the answer. **None is silently decided**: each says what this plan implements, why, and the exact bounded edit if the orchestrator or owner rules the other way. A gate should argue with these rather than discover them.

### Q-0b-1 — the `permissions_ensure` status file has a named reader in the deploy checklist, **not** in `HealthCheckService`

Spec §4.3.3's table is explicit: the ensure status file *"is read by the **wave-0b deploy checklist**, not by `HealthCheckService`: that service's `permissions_sync` check ships in wave 1 with `permissions:sync-fleet`, and adding a second key for a command wave 1 deletes would be a health check with a one-wave lifetime."*

**This plan implements the spec, with one correction rev 1 got wrong** (plan gate r1 **B0b-6**): **`permissions:ensure-fleet` does NOT write the status file.** It renders per-tenant and aggregate markers and returns an exit code — read `EnsurePermissionsFleet::handle()` in Task 4 Step 3, there is no file write in it, and there should not be: no single exit code can express *"both halves of a two-invocation deploy succeeded"*. The file is written by **`scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh`** (Deploy section), which runs both invocations under `set -euo pipefail` and writes `permissions_ensure=ok` **only when both exit 0**, else `permissions_ensure=failed:<which>`. The wave-0b deploy checklist row reads that file. `HealthCheckService` is **not** touched in 0b.

**If the orchestrator wants the admin health reader in 0b** (so the ensure deploy is observable at `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` rather than only in a shell), the bounded edit is: add a fifth check `permissions_ensure` to the array at `apps/api/app/Modules/Admin/Application/Services/HealthCheckService.php:19-24` reading that file with the same three-state rule wave 1 will use for `permissions_sync` (absent ⇒ healthy, `=ok` ⇒ healthy, `=failed` ⇒ unhealthy with an `error` string), plus one test; and wave 1 must then **delete** that key in the same commit that deletes `EnsurePermissions`. It is ~30 lines in and ~30 lines out. **Ask before adding it.**

### Q-0b-2 — which three `services.*` keys, and what gates the two DELETE routes

Spec §8 pins **three** `services.*` and **three** `service-categories.*` keys (and §4.3.0a's v0 table pins **seven** manager additions in total), while §4.4.4 counts *"six routes"* for the pair and §4.5.4 item 1 says the FE alias file's keys *"become real permissions in wave 0b"*. The alias file declares exactly `services.view`, `services.create`, `services.edit` (`apps/web/src/hooks/uiAliasPermissions.ts:8-10`), and `MODULE_PERMISSIONS.services` is `['services.view']` (`apps/web/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts:80`) under a **fail-closed** contract — so a wave-0b catalogue without `services.view` makes the `/services` nav entry vanish for everyone the moment wave 2a deletes the fallback.

**This plan implements** `services.{view,create,update}` and `service-categories.{view,create,update}` — the alias map's three, with `edit` canonicalised to `update` per D5 — which keeps §4.5.4 item 1 true and keeps the seven-key arithmetic exact. **`DELETE /services/{service}` and `DELETE /service-categories/{category}` are gated on `can:services.update` / `can:service-categories.update`**, on the ground that retiring a service catalogue row is the same authority as editing it, and that a fourth key would change three counts the spec pins in three different places.

**If the owner rules that deletion is its own authority**, the bounded edit is: add `services.delete` and `service-categories.delete` (21 keys total, **nine** manager additions), change those two route gates, and update `LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions()`, the deploy `--keys` list and `Wave0bAdditionConstantTest` in the same commit. Nothing else moves.

### Q-0b-3 — the four `channels.*` verbs

Spec §8 pins **four** `channels.*` keys and names no verbs. The tenant-facing group has ten routes: four reads (`index`, `orders` all, `{id}/sync-operations`, `{id}/orders`) and six writes (`store`, `{id}/test-connection`, `{id}/resync`, `{channelId}/orders/{orderId}/promote`, `{channelId}/products/{productId}/publish`, `{channelId}/products/{productId}/unpublish`).

**This plan implements** `channels.view` (the four reads), `channels.create` (`store`), `channels.update` (`test-connection`, `resync` — configuration writes on an existing channel), `channels.operate` (`promote`, `publish`, `unpublish` — the day-to-day channel operations, and `Operate` is a case of the closed verb enum wave 1 introduces). If the orchestrator prefers `promote` under a fifth key (it converts a channel order into a sales order), that is one key and one route gate.

### Q-0b-4 — the 0b deploy is **two** invocations of `permissions:ensure-fleet`, not one

Spec §8's deploy row says *"one command over the whole fleet, with the 0b key list and the template roles each key is declared for"*. The signature it specifies cannot express that: `--keys=*` and `--grant-to=*` are two flat lists, and the contract of `--grant-to` is *"role names that must hold **every** named key"*. Twelve of the nineteen keys are `admin`-only, so a single invocation carrying all nineteen with `--grant-to=manager` would grant a manager twelve keys the spec says are admin-only.

**This plan implements two invocations** of the same fleet command — one carrying the twelve admin-only keys with **no** `--grant-to`, one carrying the seven with `--grant-to=manager --grant-to=general_manager`. The seven land in **one** run, which is what `EnsurePermissionsCustomisationGuardTest::all_seven_additions_land_on_a_pristine_manager_in_one_run` requires (the eligibility snapshot is taken once per role, before any of that role's additions are written). The alternative — a per-key role map on the command line — is more machinery than a command wave 1 deletes deserves.

**Two invocations need ONE success condition, so they are wrapped** (plan gate r1 **B0b-6**). `scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh` runs `invocation 1 && invocation 2` under `set -euo pipefail` and is the only thing that writes the status file. Rev 1 listed the two commands and a separate *"write the status file on success"* step with no executable definition of *success*, under which a failed first invocation followed by a successful second one would have recorded a healthy deploy over a fleet missing twelve keys. The wrapper is written out in the **Deploy** section and is committed by Task 14.

## Task 1 (0b-9a): `PermissionRenameMap`, `LegacyRoleBaseline` and its two predicates

**Why this is first.** Everything else in 0b that writes a grant needs an oracle for *"is this role still what the lane left it"*, and that oracle is a pure function over arrays. It has no database dependency, no `roles` column dependency and no command dependency, so it can be built and proven before any writer exists.

**The correction this task applies, stated up front.** Spec §8 lists `PermissionRenameMap` under **wave 1**. It cannot be: `matchesPreWave0b()` normalises through the rename map on **both** sides (§4.3.0a rule 1's ruling), and `matchesPreWave0b()` ships in 0b because `permissions:ensure` is its only caller. The map is therefore a **0b** deliverable that wave 1 inherits unchanged, exactly as `LegacyRoleBaseline` and `PermissionWriteLock` already are. Wave 1's plan must not re-create it.

**Files**

- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/PermissionRenameMap.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/LegacyRoleBaseline.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Console/Commands/DumpLegacyRoleBaseline.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Fixtures/Identity/legacy-role-baseline-v0.json` (generated, then committed)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionRenameMapTest.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/LegacyRoleBaselineParityTest.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/LegacyRoleBaselinePredicateTest.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/Wave0bAdditionConstantTest.php`

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::rolePermissionGrants()` — the lane's `public static`, alive until wave 1 turns it into a registry shim.
- Produces:
  - `PermissionRenameMap::entries(): list<array{from: non-empty-string, to: non-empty-string, introducedIn: non-empty-string}>`
  - `PermissionRenameMap::sources(): list<non-empty-string>`, `::targets(): list<non-empty-string>`, `::to(string): ?string`
  - `PermissionRenameMap::canonicalise(list<string>): list<string>` — **single pass**, deduplicated, sorted
  - `LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants(): array<string, list<string>>` (static)
  - `LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions(string $templateKey): list<string>` (static)
  - `LegacyRoleBaseline::version0(string $templateKey): list<string>` (static)
  - `LegacyRoleBaseline::matchesPreWave0b(string $templateKey, array $currentKeys): bool` (instance — wave-0b `ensure` oracle **only**)
  - `LegacyRoleBaseline::matchesVersion0(string $templateKey, array $currentKeys): bool` (instance — wave-1 adoption oracle **only**)

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write the rename map.** The eleven entries are §4.9.2's table verbatim; each `from` was verified present in the lane's seeder and each `to` verified absent from it (Phase 0 re-derives the lines).

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization;

/**
 * The catalogue's permission-key rename map (owner ruling D5, spec 4.9.2).
 *
 * APPEND-ONLY and SHRINK-NEVER: an entry is removed only once
 * `permissions:sync --dry-run` reports it stale on every tenant, which is why
 * the sync reports staleness at all.
 *
 * Two invariants make the single-pass canonicalisation below a fixed point,
 * and both are asserted by PermissionRenameMapTest:
 *   - the map is acyclic;
 *   - the TARGET set and the SOURCE set are DISJOINT. Acyclicity alone is not
 *     enough: an acyclic map may still contain the chain a->b, b->c, under
 *     which one pass gives {b} and two passes give {c}, so the comparison's
 *     answer would depend on how many times it had been normalised.
 *
 * A rename is applied IN PLACE by the wave-1 sync (UPDATE permissions SET name),
 * so no grant pivot is ever rewritten. This class only names the pairs.
 */
final readonly class PermissionRenameMap
{
    /**
     * @return list<array{from: non-empty-string, to: non-empty-string, introducedIn: non-empty-string}>
     */
    public function entries(): array
    {
        return [
            // snake_case resource -> kebab-case resource (D5)
            ['from' => 'catalog_cart.view', 'to' => 'catalog-cart.view', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'catalog_cart.create', 'to' => 'catalog-cart.create', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'catalog_cart.convert_po', 'to' => 'catalog-cart.convert_po', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'catalog_cart.convert_so', 'to' => 'catalog-cart.convert_so', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'catalog_cart.marketplace_checkout', 'to' => 'catalog-cart.marketplace_checkout', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'catalog_cart.manage_all', 'to' => 'catalog-cart.manage_all', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'pos_held_orders.view', 'to' => 'pos-held-orders.view', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'pos_held_orders.create', 'to' => 'pos-held-orders.create', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'pos_held_orders.delete', 'to' => 'pos-held-orders.delete', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            // edit -> update verb normalisation (D5)
            ['from' => 'uom.edit', 'to' => 'uom.update', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
            ['from' => 'deliveries.edit', 'to' => 'deliveries.update', 'introducedIn' => '2026.09'],
        ];
    }

    /** @return array<non-empty-string, non-empty-string> */
    public function map(): array
    {
        $map = [];
        foreach ($this->entries() as $entry) {
            $map[$entry['from']] = $entry['to'];
        }

        return $map;
    }

    /** @return list<non-empty-string> */
    public function sources(): array
    {
        return array_values(array_keys($this->map()));
    }

    /** @return list<non-empty-string> */
    public function targets(): array
    {
        return array_values($this->map());
    }

    public function to(string $key): ?string
    {
        return $this->map()[$key] ?? null;
    }

    /**
     * canonical(S) = { renameMap.to(k) ?? k : k in S }, applied in EXACTLY ONE
     * pass, deduplicated and sorted so the result is comparable as a set with
     * assertSame(). Total, order-free and — under the disjointness invariant
     * above — idempotent.
     *
     * @param  list<string>  $keys
     * @return list<string>
     */
    public function canonicalise(array $keys): array
    {
        $map = $this->map();
        $canonical = [];
        foreach ($keys as $key) {
            $canonical[$map[$key] ?? $key] = true;
        }
        $canonical = array_keys($canonical);
        sort($canonical);

        return $canonical;
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 2 — write the fixture generator, and run it once.** The fixture is generated from the live lane method, never hand-copied.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Console\Commands;

use Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder;
use Illuminate\Console\Command;
use Illuminate\Filesystem\Filesystem;

/**
 * Freeze RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::rolePermissionGrants() — the POST-A-1a
 * grant map — into the committed fixture LegacyRoleBaseline reads.
 *
 * Run ONCE, in wave 0b, while the lane's `public static` methods still exist.
 * Wave 1 turns those statics into registry shims, at which point
 * LegacyRoleBaselineParityTest re-points from a direct call to this fixture and
 * this command is deleted (spec 4.3.0a rule 1).
 */
final class DumpLegacyRoleBaseline extends Command
{
    protected $signature = 'permissions:dump-baseline
        {--path= : Override the fixture output path}';

    protected $description = 'Freeze the post-A-1a role grant map into the legacy-role-baseline fixture.';

    public function __construct(private readonly Filesystem $files)
    {
        parent::__construct();
    }

    public function handle(): int
    {
        $pathOption = $this->option('path');
        $path = is_string($pathOption) && $pathOption !== ''
            ? $pathOption
            : base_path('tests/Fixtures/Identity/legacy-role-baseline-v0.json');

        $grants = RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::rolePermissionGrants();
        ksort($grants);
        foreach ($grants as $role => $keys) {
            $unique = array_values(array_unique($keys));
            sort($unique);
            $grants[$role] = $unique;
        }

        $json = json_encode($grants, JSON_PRETTY_PRINT | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES | JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR)."\n";

        $this->files->ensureDirectoryExists(dirname($path));
        if ($this->files->put($path, $json) === false) {
            $this->error('Unable to write '.$path.'.');

            return self::FAILURE;
        }

        $this->info('Wrote '.count($grants).' template rows to '.$path.'.');

        return self::SUCCESS;
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 3 — write `LegacyRoleBaseline`.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization;

use RuntimeException;

/**
 * The frozen version-0 baseline of the eight seeded role templates, and the TWO
 * equality oracles built on it. Ships in wave 0b (spec 4.3.5's rev-6 B-1
 * ruling); wave 1's adoption pass INHERITS it rather than introducing it.
 *
 * The two predicates measure the role at two DIFFERENT MOMENTS and must never
 * collapse into one:
 *
 *   matchesPreWave0b(k, S)  <=>  canonical(S) == canonical(postA1aGrants(k))
 *       "the role is still exactly what lane/w-lot-a-1a left it" — the ONLY
 *       oracle `permissions:ensure` may use, because it runs BEFORE the 0b
 *       additions are granted.
 *
 *   matchesVersion0(k, S)   <=>  canonical(S) == canonical(version0(k))
 *       version0 = postA1aGrants ∪ wave0bAdditions — the ONLY oracle wave-1
 *       adoption may use, because it runs AFTER them.
 *
 * Using matchesVersion0() inside `ensure` is unsatisfiable by construction: on a
 * pristine post-A-1a manager, current = P while version0 = P ∪ A, so the very
 * first wave-0b grant would be unreachable. LegacyRoleBaselinePredicateTest
 * pins that the pair does not collapse.
 *
 * Both normalise through PermissionRenameMap on BOTH sides, so neither the
 * mandatory wave-1 rename nor a legitimate 0b grant can make a pristine role
 * compare unequal.
 */
final readonly class LegacyRoleBaseline
{
    private const FIXTURE = 'tests/Fixtures/Identity/legacy-role-baseline-v0.json';

    /**
     * What wave 0b ADDS to each template's v0 grant set. A committed constant,
     * not a computation: every element is known at the moment 0b ships, which is
     * what makes `version0 = postA1a ∪ additions` safe.
     *
     * `admin` CARRIES ALL NINETEEN (spec 4.3.0a rule 1's v0 table, row `admin`:
     * "all 19 0b keys"). This is the wave-1 ADOPTION oracle's input, and it is
     * NOT the same list as the deploy's `--grant-to` arguments:
     *
     *   - EnsurePermissionsRunner grants `admin` every key in --keys
     *     UNCONDITIONALLY, on every invocation, without ever being named in
     *     --grant-to (clause 2 of its contract). After the 0b deploy a pristine
     *     admin therefore HOLDS all nineteen.
     *   - So version0('admin') MUST include them. Declaring admin's additions as
     *     [] — rev 1 of the 0b plan did, and asserted it in a test — makes an
     *     ensure-provisioned PRISTINE admin compare UNEQUAL to v0 during wave-1
     *     adoption and be classified `adopted_customised`: the opposite of the
     *     eight-pristine-role state 4.3.0a requires, and the opposite of what
     *     PermissionsSyncSupersedesEnsureTest expects on the first sync of an
     *     ensure-provisioned tenant. (Plan gate r1, BLOCKER B0b-1.)
     *   - Only `manager` and `general_manager` are ever passed as --grant-to,
     *     because only they need an EQUALITY GUARD before receiving a key.
     *     Wave0bAdditionConstantTest asserts BOTH properties separately:
     *     admin == the nineteen, and the grant-to roles == the seven the deploy
     *     actually types on the command line.
     *
     * That admin is also re-derived to activeKeys() by wave 1's sync step 8 is
     * true and is a SECOND safety net; it is not a licence for this row to be
     * wrong, because adoption (step 4) runs BEFORE step 8 and its verdict is
     * what writes `customised_at`.
     */
    public const WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS = [
        // All nineteen — every key this wave declares. Admin is granted
        // unconditionally, so its v0 set grows by the whole list.
        'admin' => [
            'credit-notes.cancel',
            'services.view',
            'services.create',
            'services.update',
            'service-categories.view',
            'service-categories.create',
            'service-categories.update',
            'channels.view',
            'channels.create',
            'channels.update',
            'channels.operate',
            'categories.view',
            'categories.create',
            'categories.update',
            'categories.delete',
            'progression.view',
            'progression.manage',
            'purchase-hub.orders.create',
            'companies.create',
        ],
        'manager' => [
            'credit-notes.cancel',
            'services.view',
            'services.create',
            'services.update',
            'service-categories.view',
            'service-categories.create',
            'service-categories.update',
        ],
        'general_manager' => [
            'credit-notes.cancel',
            'services.view',
            'services.create',
            'services.update',
            'service-categories.view',
            'service-categories.create',
            'service-categories.update',
        ],
    ];

    public function __construct(private PermissionRenameMap $renameMap) {}

    /**
     * The post-A-1a grant map, read from the committed fixture.
     *
     * @return array<string, list<string>>
     */
    public static function postA1aGrants(): array
    {
        $path = base_path(self::FIXTURE);
        if (! is_file($path)) {
            throw new RuntimeException('legacy role baseline fixture is missing: '.$path);
        }

        /** @var array<string, list<string>> $decoded */
        $decoded = json_decode((string) file_get_contents($path), true, 512, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR);

        return $decoded;
    }

    /** @return list<string> */
    public static function wave0bAdditions(string $templateKey): array
    {
        return self::WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS[$templateKey] ?? [];
    }

    /**
     * version0(k) = postA1aGrants(k) ∪ wave0bAdditions(k), in the RAW
     * post-A-1a spelling. Normalisation happens on read, in the predicates.
     *
     * @return list<string>
     */
    public static function version0(string $templateKey): array
    {
        $base = self::postA1aGrants()[$templateKey] ?? [];

        return array_values(array_unique([...$base, ...self::wave0bAdditions($templateKey)]));
    }

    /** @param list<string> $currentKeys */
    public function matchesPreWave0b(string $templateKey, array $currentKeys): bool
    {
        $baseline = self::postA1aGrants()[$templateKey] ?? null;
        if ($baseline === null) {
            return false;
        }

        return $this->renameMap->canonicalise($currentKeys) === $this->renameMap->canonicalise($baseline);
    }

    /** @param list<string> $currentKeys */
    public function matchesVersion0(string $templateKey, array $currentKeys): bool
    {
        if (! array_key_exists($templateKey, self::postA1aGrants())) {
            return false;
        }

        return $this->renameMap->canonicalise($currentKeys) === $this->renameMap->canonicalise(self::version0($templateKey));
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 4 — the four tests.** Write them **before** running them; each must fail for a stated reason first (delete an entry, mistype a key), and the first failing assertion text goes in the handback.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Identity;

use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRenameMap;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\TestCase;

final class PermissionRenameMapTest extends TestCase
{
    #[Test]
    public function it_declares_exactly_the_eleven_ruled_entries(): void
    {
        $map = new PermissionRenameMap;

        $this->assertCount(11, $map->entries(), 'spec 4.9.2 pins eleven rename pairs; PermissionsSyncSupersedesEnsureTest expects renamed=11 in wave 1.');
        $this->assertSame([
            'catalog_cart.view' => 'catalog-cart.view',
            'catalog_cart.create' => 'catalog-cart.create',
            'catalog_cart.convert_po' => 'catalog-cart.convert_po',
            'catalog_cart.convert_so' => 'catalog-cart.convert_so',
            'catalog_cart.marketplace_checkout' => 'catalog-cart.marketplace_checkout',
            'catalog_cart.manage_all' => 'catalog-cart.manage_all',
            'pos_held_orders.view' => 'pos-held-orders.view',
            'pos_held_orders.create' => 'pos-held-orders.create',
            'pos_held_orders.delete' => 'pos-held-orders.delete',
            'uom.edit' => 'uom.update',
            'deliveries.edit' => 'deliveries.update',
        ], $map->map());
    }

    /**
     * The invariant that makes the single-pass canonicalisation a fixed point.
     * Appending b->c beside an existing a->b fails HERE, at build time, which
     * is the moment a chain could first be introduced.
     */
    #[Test]
    public function rename_targets_are_not_sources(): void
    {
        $map = new PermissionRenameMap;

        $this->assertSame(
            [],
            array_values(array_intersect($map->targets(), $map->sources())),
            'a rename target that is also a rename source is a chain; single-pass canonical() would stop depending on how often it had been applied.',
        );
    }

    #[Test]
    public function the_map_is_acyclic(): void
    {
        $map = new PermissionRenameMap;
        foreach ($map->map() as $from => $to) {
            $seen = [$from => true];
            $cursor = $to;
            while ($cursor !== null && ! isset($seen[$cursor])) {
                $seen[$cursor] = true;
                $cursor = $map->to($cursor);
            }
            $this->assertNull($cursor, "rename cycle reached from '{$from}'.");
        }
    }

    #[Test]
    public function canonicalise_is_a_fixed_point_and_order_free(): void
    {
        $map = new PermissionRenameMap;
        $once = $map->canonicalise(['uom.edit', 'products.view', 'catalog_cart.view']);
        $twice = $map->canonicalise($once);

        $this->assertSame($once, $twice);
        $this->assertSame(['catalog-cart.view', 'products.view', 'uom.update'], $once);
        $this->assertSame($once, $map->canonicalise(['catalog_cart.view', 'uom.edit', 'products.view']));
    }

    #[Test]
    public function no_rename_source_is_reachable_from_a_catalogue_key_the_seeder_still_declares_as_a_target(): void
    {
        $map = new PermissionRenameMap;
        $catalogue = \Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::permissionNames();

        foreach ($map->targets() as $target) {
            $this->assertNotContains(
                $target,
                $catalogue,
                "the seeder already declares '{$target}', so the rename would be BLOCKED with reason=rename_target_exists on every tenant.",
            );
        }
        foreach ($map->sources() as $source) {
            $this->assertContains(
                $source,
                $catalogue,
                "rename source '{$source}' is not in the catalogue — the entry is stale and must be trimmed, not carried.",
            );
        }
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Identity;

use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\LegacyRoleBaseline;
use Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\TestCase;

/**
 * The fixture is a GENERATED copy of the lane's live method, not a
 * transcription. While wave 0b runs, both still exist, so the assertion is a
 * direct call. Wave 1 turns the statics into registry shims and RE-POINTS this
 * test at the fixture in the same commit (spec 4.3.0a rule 1).
 */
final class LegacyRoleBaselineParityTest extends TestCase
{
    #[Test]
    public function the_fixture_is_the_lane_method_verbatim(): void
    {
        $live = RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::rolePermissionGrants();
        $frozen = LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants();

        $this->assertSame(array_keys($this->normalise($live)), array_keys($this->normalise($frozen)));
        $this->assertSame($this->normalise($live), $this->normalise($frozen));
    }

    #[Test]
    public function it_carries_the_eight_post_lane_templates(): void
    {
        $this->assertSame(
            ['accountant', 'admin', 'cashier', 'general_manager', 'manager', 'operator', 'technician', 'viewer'],
            array_keys(LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants()),
            'seven rows means lane/w-lot-a-1a has not merged; the fixture must be regenerated after it does.',
        );
    }

    /**
     * @param  array<string, list<string>>  $grants
     * @return array<string, list<string>>
     */
    private function normalise(array $grants): array
    {
        ksort($grants);
        foreach ($grants as $role => $keys) {
            $unique = array_values(array_unique($keys));
            sort($unique);
            $grants[$role] = $unique;
        }

        return $grants;
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Identity;

use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\LegacyRoleBaseline;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRenameMap;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\TestCase;

/**
 * The two oracles must NOT collapse. Rev 5 of the spec had `permissions:ensure`
 * call matchesVersion0() before granting the additions, which is unsatisfiable
 * by construction — the first 0b grant would have been unreachable and every
 * manager would have been classified customised.
 */
final class LegacyRoleBaselinePredicateTest extends TestCase
{
    private LegacyRoleBaseline $baseline;

    protected function setUp(): void
    {
        parent::setUp();
        $this->baseline = new LegacyRoleBaseline(new PermissionRenameMap);
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_pristine_post_lane_manager_is_pre_wave_0b_but_not_version_0(): void
    {
        $pristine = LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants()['manager'];

        $this->assertTrue($this->baseline->matchesPreWave0b('manager', $pristine));
        $this->assertFalse($this->baseline->matchesVersion0('manager', $pristine));
    }

    #[Test]
    public function after_the_seven_additions_it_is_version_0_but_no_longer_pre_wave_0b(): void
    {
        $granted = [...LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants()['manager'], ...LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions('manager')];

        $this->assertFalse($this->baseline->matchesPreWave0b('manager', $granted));
        $this->assertTrue($this->baseline->matchesVersion0('manager', $granted));
    }

    #[Test]
    public function the_rename_is_absorbed_on_both_sides(): void
    {
        $renamed = array_map(
            static fn (string $key): string => match ($key) {
                'uom.edit' => 'uom.update',
                'deliveries.edit' => 'deliveries.update',
                default => $key,
            },
            LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants()['manager'],
        );

        $this->assertTrue(
            $this->baseline->matchesPreWave0b('manager', $renamed),
            'a role whose keys have already been renamed in place is still pristine; canonical() absorbs the rename on both sides.',
        );
    }

    #[Test]
    public function one_extra_grant_and_one_missing_grant_both_break_equality(): void
    {
        $pristine = LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants()['manager'];

        $this->assertFalse($this->baseline->matchesPreWave0b('manager', [...$pristine, 'settings.manage']));
        $this->assertFalse($this->baseline->matchesPreWave0b('manager', array_slice($pristine, 1)));
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_unknown_template_key_is_never_pristine(): void
    {
        $this->assertFalse($this->baseline->matchesPreWave0b('warehouse_supervisor', []));
        $this->assertFalse($this->baseline->matchesVersion0('warehouse_supervisor', []));
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Identity;

use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\LegacyRoleBaseline;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\TestCase;

/**
 * TWO properties, deliberately asserted apart (plan gate r1 B0b-1).
 *
 *  (1) WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS is the v0 ARITHMETIC input: what each template's grant
 *      set gains in wave 0b, whatever mechanism delivers it. `admin` gains all
 *      nineteen, because EnsurePermissionsRunner grants admin every key in
 *      --keys unconditionally.
 *  (2) The DEPLOY's `--grant-to` arguments are a strict subset: the seven keys
 *      and the two roles that need an equality guard. Admin never appears in
 *      --grant-to and does not need to.
 *
 * If (1) and the runner ever drift, a pristine admin compares unequal to v0 in
 * wave 1 and is permanently classified customised — silently. If (2) and the
 * deploy drift, a manager receives a key the adoption oracle does not expect,
 * with the same silent consequence.
 *
 * THE CATALOGUE-FACING CASES ARE NOT IN THIS FILE'S FIRST COMMIT. They assert
 * that every key is declared in the seeder, which is Task 5's and Task 6's work,
 * and a test that is knowingly red is not an independently mergeable commit
 * (plan gate r1 B0b-2). Task 6 Step 4 ADDS `Wave0bAdditionCatalogueTest` in the
 * commit that declares the last of the nineteen keys.
 */
final class Wave0bAdditionConstantTest extends TestCase
{
    /** The nineteen keys wave 0b declares, in `--keys` order. */
    private const ALL_NINETEEN = [
        'credit-notes.cancel',
        'services.view',
        'services.create',
        'services.update',
        'service-categories.view',
        'service-categories.create',
        'service-categories.update',
        'channels.view',
        'channels.create',
        'channels.update',
        'channels.operate',
        'categories.view',
        'categories.create',
        'categories.update',
        'categories.delete',
        'progression.view',
        'progression.manage',
        'purchase-hub.orders.create',
        'companies.create',
    ];

    /** The exact `--grant-to` invocation this wave's deploy runs (plan Task 14, Q-0b-4 run 2). */
    private const DEPLOYED_GRANT_KEYS = [
        'credit-notes.cancel',
        'services.view',
        'services.create',
        'services.update',
        'service-categories.view',
        'service-categories.create',
        'service-categories.update',
    ];

    private const DEPLOYED_GRANT_ROLES = ['manager', 'general_manager'];

    #[Test]
    public function admin_v0_gains_all_nineteen_because_ensure_grants_admin_unconditionally(): void
    {
        $this->assertSame(
            self::ALL_NINETEEN,
            LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions('admin'),
            'spec 4.3.0a rule 1 pins admin\'s v0 additions as all nineteen. An empty list here makes an '
            .'ensure-provisioned PRISTINE admin adopt as `adopted_customised` in wave 1.',
        );
    }

    #[Test]
    public function the_grant_to_constant_equals_the_deployed_arguments(): void
    {
        foreach (self::DEPLOYED_GRANT_ROLES as $role) {
            $this->assertSame(
                self::DEPLOYED_GRANT_KEYS,
                LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions($role),
                "wave0bAdditions('{$role}') must equal the keys the deploy actually grants that role.",
            );
        }
    }

    #[Test]
    public function the_deployed_grant_keys_are_a_strict_subset_of_the_nineteen(): void
    {
        $this->assertSame(
            [],
            array_values(array_diff(self::DEPLOYED_GRANT_KEYS, self::ALL_NINETEEN)),
            'the deploy cannot grant a key this wave does not declare.',
        );
        $this->assertNotSame(self::DEPLOYED_GRANT_KEYS, self::ALL_NINETEEN, 'twelve keys are admin-only.');
    }

    #[Test]
    public function no_template_but_admin_manager_and_general_manager_gains_anything(): void
    {
        foreach (['cashier', 'viewer', 'technician', 'operator', 'accountant'] as $role) {
            $this->assertSame([], LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions($role));
        }
    }

    #[Test]
    public function version0_is_the_union_and_admin_is_the_widest_row(): void
    {
        $adminV0 = LegacyRoleBaseline::version0('admin');
        foreach (self::ALL_NINETEEN as $key) {
            $this->assertContains($key, $adminV0);
        }
        $this->assertGreaterThan(
            count(LegacyRoleBaseline::version0('manager')),
            count($adminV0),
            'admin holds every key manager does, plus the twelve admin-only 0b keys.',
        );
    }
}
~~~

> **Every case in this file is green the moment `LegacyRoleBaseline` exists** — they are all pure constant arithmetic with no seeder dependency. That is the point of the split: this task's commit is green in isolation (plan gate r1 **B0b-2**). The catalogue-facing property — *"and every one of the nineteen is actually declared in `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`"* — needs Task 5 and Task 6 and therefore ships **with** them, as `Wave0bAdditionCatalogueTest` in Task 6 Step 4.

## Task 2 (0b-9b): `PermissionWriteLock`

**What it is, and what it is not.** One transaction-scoped PostgreSQL advisory lock per tenant, serialising every writer of a `role_has_permissions` or `model_has_roles` row. It is **not** a table lock — ordinary reads are never blocked — and `pg_advisory_xact_lock` releases on commit **or rollback**, which is what makes a failed run safe to retry.

**The key is adopted, not invented.** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` already ships a role-grant writer that locks `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId` hashed with `hashtextextended(?, 0)` (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:131-136`) and locks its roles by `name` then `id` (`:111-116`). A second namespace would be a second serialization domain — i.e. **no** serialization between the two writers — and for `manager` versus `general_manager` the `name` and `id` orders can be opposite, which is a deadlock rather than a race. The lane lands first, so this design **adopts**. The key string keeps the lane's name even after wave 3 retires the delta as dead code: an advisory key is an opaque token whose only property is that every writer computes the same one.

**Files**

- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Infrastructure/Locks/PermissionWriteLock.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionWriteLockContractTest.php`

**Interfaces**

- Produces: `PermissionWriteLock::acquire(string $tenantId): void` and `PermissionWriteLock::key(string $tenantId): string`.
- Consumed by: `EnsurePermissionsRunner` (Task 3), `RoleController` ×5, `UserController` ×2, `ResetTenantCommand` (Task 4), and — in wave 1 — `PermissionSyncService` and `TemplateDeltaApplier`.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write the helper.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Locks;

use Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB;
use LogicException;

/**
 * Serialises every role-grant writer for ONE tenant, for the life of the
 * CURRENT transaction.
 *
 * The key and the hash function are lane/w-lot-a-1a's, adopted VERBATIM so that
 * LotActionPermissionDelta::apply() and every writer wired in wave 0b share ONE
 * serialization domain (see that class's acquireTenantLock()). Two advisory
 * namespaces would be two domains, i.e. no serialization at all.
 *
 * Companion obligations, on the CALLER (spec 4.3.2's five obligations):
 *   (i)   open a transaction;
 *   (ii)  call acquire() as its FIRST statement;
 *   (iii) SELECT ... FOR UPDATE the affected roles rows ordered by
 *         roles.name ASC, then roles.id ASC — the lane's order, because a
 *         deterministic order is only a deadlock cure when every participant
 *         uses the SAME one;
 *   (iv)  RE-READ the decision inputs (grant set, and from wave 1 customised_at)
 *         AFTER the lock is held, and decide from that read;
 *   (v)   write and commit.
 *
 * The ONE stated exception is RoleController::store, which creates a row that
 * does not yet exist: advisory lock -> INSERT -> initial grants, in one
 * transaction, with no FOR UPDATE and no prior grant set to re-read. A
 * post-insert FOR UPDATE on a row the same transaction just created adds no
 * serialization beyond the advisory lock it already holds, so it is not
 * required and must not be added as ritual.
 *
 * PostgreSQL: SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(<key>, 0))
 * SQLite:     no-op (the fast lane is single-connection; the serialization
 *             property is asserted on the PG lane only — EC-20a/b/c).
 */
final readonly class PermissionWriteLock
{
    public static function key(string $tenantId): string
    {
        return 'wlota1a:'.$tenantId;
    }

    public function acquire(string $tenantId): void
    {
        if (DB::transactionLevel() === 0) {
            throw new LogicException(
                'PermissionWriteLock::acquire() must be called inside an open transaction — '
                .'a transaction-scoped advisory lock taken outside one is released immediately.',
            );
        }

        if (DB::getDriverName() !== 'pgsql') {
            return;
        }

        DB::select('SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(?, 0))', [self::key($tenantId)]);
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 2 — write the contract test.** The transaction assertion is driver-independent on purpose: it makes the contract meaningful on the fast lane, where the lock itself is a no-op.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Identity;

use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Locks\PermissionWriteLock;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB;
use LogicException;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\TestCase;

final class PermissionWriteLockContractTest extends TestCase
{
    #[Test]
    public function the_key_is_the_lane_key_verbatim(): void
    {
        $this->assertSame(
            'wlota1a:8f1d2f7e-0000-4000-8000-000000000001',
            PermissionWriteLock::key('8f1d2f7e-0000-4000-8000-000000000001'),
            'the advisory key is adopted from lane/w-lot-a-1a; renaming it splits the serialization domain in two.',
        );
    }

    #[Test]
    public function acquiring_outside_a_transaction_is_a_logic_error(): void
    {
        $this->expectException(LogicException::class);
        $this->expectExceptionMessageMatches('/inside an open transaction/');

        (new PermissionWriteLock)->acquire('8f1d2f7e-0000-4000-8000-000000000001');
    }

    #[Test]
    public function inside_a_transaction_it_is_a_no_op_on_sqlite_and_one_statement_on_postgres(): void
    {
        DB::enableQueryLog();
        DB::transaction(function (): void {
            (new PermissionWriteLock)->acquire('8f1d2f7e-0000-4000-8000-000000000001');
        });
        $log = DB::getQueryLog();
        DB::disableQueryLog();

        $advisory = array_values(array_filter(
            $log,
            static fn (array $entry): bool => str_contains((string) $entry['query'], 'pg_advisory_xact_lock'),
        ));

        if (DB::getDriverName() === 'pgsql') {
            $this->assertCount(1, $advisory);
            $this->assertStringContainsString('hashtextextended(?, 0)', (string) $advisory[0]['query']);
            $this->assertSame(['wlota1a:8f1d2f7e-0000-4000-8000-000000000001'], $advisory[0]['bindings']);
        } else {
            $this->assertSame([], $advisory, 'the helper is a documented no-op off PostgreSQL.');
        }
    }
}
~~~
