## Task 3 (0b-9c, part 1): `TenantFleetRunner` — the one fleet iterator

**Why a class and not `tenants:run`.** Three defects rule `tenants:run` out (spec §4.3.3): child options must be forwarded as `--option=['k=v']` and a bare child flag errors; `tenants:run` **discards child exit statuses**; and the entrypoint additionally catches failures and keeps booting. A half-synced fleet would be logged as completed while the app started serving mixed authorization state. There is therefore **exactly one** runner, shared by `permissions:ensure-fleet` (this wave) and `permissions:sync-fleet` (wave 1), because the universe predicates, the failure semantics, the per-tenant marker shape and the exit contract are the parts a gate has to re-verify and they are written **once**.

**Gate r9 m-1 is applied here.** The start snapshot is `get(['id', 'slug'])`, **not** `pluck('id', 'slug')` — `pluck` collapses duplicate keys rather than preserving a match set, and it cannot represent a cross-column collision at all. `tenants.slug` **is** globally unique (`apps/api/database/migrations/2025_11_30_000001_create_tenants_table.php:19`), so the reachable ambiguity is a selector equal to tenant A's **UUID** and tenant B's **slug** — never two equal slugs, which the schema forbids.

**Files**

- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Domain/Enums/FleetOutcome.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/DTOs/FleetTenantOutcome.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/DTOs/FleetRunResult.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenantFleetRunner.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Tenant/TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest.php`

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant`, Stancl `tenancy()`, the tenant migration path `database/migrations/tenant`.
- Produces: `TenantFleetRunner::run(array $onlyTenants, string $markerPrefix, \Closure $perTenant): FleetRunResult`, where `$perTenant` is `\Closure(Tenant): FleetTenantOutcome`.
- The **command** — never the runner — renders the aggregate marker line and returns the process exit code.

**Contract, restated so a reviewer can check it against the code**

1. **One start read defines the run's UNIVERSE.** `$directoryAtStart = Tenant::query()->get(['id', 'slug'])`. That set is the run's clock: **no later read may add a tenant to it or remove one from it.** Two later reads exist and are deliberate (rev 2 states them, because rev 1's "nothing later re-reads it except step 4" was false of the code — plan gate r1 minor): (a) each **selected** tenant is re-hydrated with `Tenant::query()->find($id)` immediately before it is processed, because the snapshot carries only `id` and `slug` while `runOne()` needs the full model to resolve a database name — and a row that **vanished** between the snapshot and now is `FAILED` with `tenant_not_ready:directory_row_disappeared`, never silently dropped; (b) step 4's closing `pluck('id')`, a pure population gauge. Neither read can enlarge the universe: (a) only ever narrows it, and (b) is never iterated.
2. **Selector resolution (step 1a).** Empty `$onlyTenants` ⇒ the whole snapshot. Otherwise each selector is matched against the snapshot on `id` (exact UUID) **or** `slug` (exact, case-sensitive) — one lookup, the **union** of matches, not a sequence with a fallback. Exactly one match contributes that tenant. Duplicates **collapse** (a tenant's id and its slug in one invocation yield one tenant, processed once, counted once). Zero matches ⇒ `FAILED`, `reason=tenant_selector_unmatched`, named by the **literal selector string**. More than one ⇒ `FAILED`, `reason=tenant_selector_ambiguous`, with the matched ids listed. Either failure counts in `failed=` and makes the run exit non-zero, and per-selector isolation matches per-tenant isolation — every selector that *did* resolve is still processed.
3. **Readiness, per tenant, at the moment of use.** (a) the database **physically exists** — the same check `TenancyResolver::initializeIfProvisioned()` performs (`apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenancyResolver.php:90`); (b) after `tenancy()->initialize()`, the tenant's `migrations` table contains **every** file under `database/migrations/tenant`. A tenant failing either is **`FAILED` with `reason=tenant_not_ready`** (sub-reason `database_absent` or `migrations_behind`) and the run exits non-zero. It is *not* an informational skip: nothing observable to the runner distinguishes a tenant that is mid-provisioning from an established tenant whose rolling migration just failed, and the entrypoint makes the second case routine — `tenants:migrate-rolling --force` runs unconditionally and its failure is caught and logged (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:150-154`). A fleet run that cannot prove it synced a tenant must not report success for that tenant.
4. **Per-tenant failure isolation.** One tenant's `FAILED`/`BLOCKED` never stops the run.
5. **The closing population gauge.** `gauges.provisioned_during_run = |directory at end| \ |directory at start|`. It is a **pure population count** and asserts **nothing** about those tenants' catalogue state: the directory row is written *before* the database is created, migrated or initialised (`TenantProvisioningService`), so a row's appearance is equally consistent with a provisioning attempt that never reached its own sync. It exists so an operator reading the aggregate line can see the fleet grew mid-run.
6. **Exit rule.** Non-zero if any tenant or any selector is `BLOCKED` or `FAILED`.
7. **`--dry-run` is WRITE-free, not FAILURE-free.** It changes exactly one thing — what is written — and nothing else. Classification, per-tenant markers (`mode=DRY_RUN`), the aggregate line and the exit rule are identical to an applying run. That belongs to the **closure**, not the runner: the runner never writes anything itself.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the enum and the two DTOs.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums;

/** One tenant's (or one selector's) result inside a fleet run. */
enum FleetOutcome: string
{
    case Applied = 'APPLIED';
    case AlreadyCurrent = 'ALREADY_CURRENT';
    case Blocked = 'BLOCKED';
    case Failed = 'FAILED';

    public function isGreen(): bool
    {
        return $this === self::Applied || $this === self::AlreadyCurrent;
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs;

use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\FleetOutcome;

final readonly class FleetTenantOutcome
{
    public function __construct(
        public FleetOutcome $outcome,
        public string $reason,
        /** The tenant id, or — for a selector failure — the literal selector string. */
        public string $subject,
    ) {}

    public static function applied(string $subject, string $reason = 'applied'): self
    {
        return new self(FleetOutcome::Applied, $reason, $subject);
    }

    public static function alreadyCurrent(string $subject, string $reason = 'already_current'): self
    {
        return new self(FleetOutcome::AlreadyCurrent, $reason, $subject);
    }

    public static function blocked(string $subject, string $reason): self
    {
        return new self(FleetOutcome::Blocked, $reason, $subject);
    }

    public static function failed(string $subject, string $reason): self
    {
        return new self(FleetOutcome::Failed, $reason, $subject);
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs;

use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\FleetOutcome;

final readonly class FleetRunResult
{
    /** @param list<FleetTenantOutcome> $outcomes */
    public function __construct(
        public array $outcomes,
        public int $provisionedDuringRun,
    ) {}

    public function tenants(): int
    {
        return count($this->outcomes);
    }

    public function count(FleetOutcome $outcome): int
    {
        return count(array_filter($this->outcomes, static fn (FleetTenantOutcome $o): bool => $o->outcome === $outcome));
    }

    /** @return list<FleetTenantOutcome> */
    public function nonGreen(): array
    {
        return array_values(array_filter($this->outcomes, static fn (FleetTenantOutcome $o): bool => ! $o->outcome->isGreen()));
    }

    public function isGreen(): bool
    {
        return $this->nonGreen() === [];
    }

    /** The closing aggregate line, e.g. `PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-FLEET tenants=3 …`. */
    public function aggregateLine(string $markerPrefix): string
    {
        $line = sprintf(
            '%s-FLEET tenants=%d applied=%d already_current=%d blocked=%d failed=%d gauges.provisioned_during_run=%d',
            $markerPrefix,
            $this->tenants(),
            $this->count(FleetOutcome::Applied),
            $this->count(FleetOutcome::AlreadyCurrent),
            $this->count(FleetOutcome::Blocked),
            $this->count(FleetOutcome::Failed),
            $this->provisionedDuringRun,
        );

        foreach ($this->nonGreen() as $outcome) {
            $line .= sprintf(' [%s %s reason=%s]', $outcome->outcome->value, $outcome->subject, $outcome->reason);
        }

        return $line;
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 2 — the runner.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Tenant\Application\Services;

use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs\FleetRunResult;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs\FleetTenantOutcome;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant;
use Closure;
use Illuminate\Support\Collection;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\File;
use Throwable;

/**
 * The ONE fleet iterator (spec 4.3.3, rev-8 gate r7 M-1).
 *
 * `tenants:run` cannot be one: it forces the --option=['k=v'] encoding on every
 * child flag, and it DISCARDS child exit statuses, so a half-applied fleet
 * would be reported as clean. This class calls the per-tenant closure DIRECTLY
 * and therefore holds the real result object.
 *
 * Shared verbatim by permissions:ensure-fleet (wave 0b) and
 * permissions:sync-fleet (wave 1). Only the closure, the marker prefix and the
 * status file differ.
 */
final readonly class TenantFleetRunner
{
    /**
     * @param  list<string>                          $onlyTenants ids or slugs; empty = every directory row
     * @param  Closure(Tenant): FleetTenantOutcome   $perTenant
     */
    public function run(array $onlyTenants, string $markerPrefix, Closure $perTenant): FleetRunResult
    {
        // 1. Read the directory ONCE. This read is the run's clock.
        /** @var Collection<int, Tenant> $directoryAtStart */
        $directoryAtStart = Tenant::query()->get(['id', 'slug']);
        $startIds = $directoryAtStart->pluck('id')->all();

        // 1a. Selector resolution against the START snapshot.
        [$selected, $selectorOutcomes] = $this->resolveSelectors($directoryAtStart, $onlyTenants);

        $outcomes = $selectorOutcomes;

        foreach ($selected as $tenantId) {
            /** @var Tenant|null $tenant */
            $tenant = Tenant::query()->find($tenantId);
            if ($tenant === null) {
                // The row vanished between the snapshot and now. Fail closed:
                // the run cannot prove it processed this tenant.
                $outcomes[] = FleetTenantOutcome::failed($tenantId, 'tenant_not_ready:directory_row_disappeared');

                continue;
            }

            $outcomes[] = $this->runOne($tenant, $perTenant);
        }

        // 4. Closing directory read — a pure population gauge.
        $endIds = Tenant::query()->pluck('id')->all();
        $provisionedDuringRun = count(array_diff($endIds, $startIds));

        return new FleetRunResult(array_values($outcomes), $provisionedDuringRun);
    }

    /**
     * @param  Collection<int, Tenant>  $snapshot
     * @param  list<string>             $onlyTenants
     * @return array{0: list<string>, 1: list<FleetTenantOutcome>}
     */
    private function resolveSelectors(Collection $snapshot, array $onlyTenants): array
    {
        if ($onlyTenants === []) {
            return [$snapshot->pluck('id')->all(), []];
        }

        $selected = [];
        $failures = [];

        foreach (array_values(array_unique($onlyTenants)) as $selector) {
            // A value is offered to BOTH columns and the UNION of matches is the
            // selector's match set — never a sequence with a fallback, which
            // would silently pick the id match and hide the collision.
            $matches = $snapshot
                ->filter(static fn (Tenant $t): bool => $t->getAttribute('id') === $selector || $t->getAttribute('slug') === $selector)
                ->pluck('id')
                ->unique()
                ->values()
                ->all();

            if ($matches === []) {
                $failures[] = FleetTenantOutcome::failed($selector, 'tenant_selector_unmatched');

                continue;
            }

            if (count($matches) > 1) {
                $failures[] = FleetTenantOutcome::failed(
                    $selector,
                    'tenant_selector_ambiguous:'.implode('|', $matches),
                );

                continue;
            }

            // Duplicates COLLAPSE: a tenant named twice (by id and by slug) is
            // processed once and counted once, or applied= would disagree with
            // the fleet size.
            $selected[$matches[0]] = true;
        }

        return [array_keys($selected), $failures];
    }

    /** @param Closure(Tenant): FleetTenantOutcome $perTenant */
    private function runOne(Tenant $tenant, Closure $perTenant): FleetTenantOutcome
    {
        $tenantId = (string) $tenant->getAttribute('id');

        try {
            // (a) the database physically exists — the same check
            // TenancyResolver::initializeIfProvisioned() performs.
            if (! $tenant->database()->manager()->databaseExists($tenant->getDatabaseName())) {
                return FleetTenantOutcome::failed($tenantId, 'tenant_not_ready:database_absent');
            }

            tenancy()->initialize($tenant);

            // (b) migrations are current.
            $pending = $this->pendingTenantMigrations();
            if ($pending !== []) {
                return FleetTenantOutcome::failed(
                    $tenantId,
                    'tenant_not_ready:migrations_behind:'.count($pending),
                );
            }

            return $perTenant($tenant);
        } catch (Throwable $exception) {
            return FleetTenantOutcome::failed($tenantId, 'exception:'.$exception->getMessage());
        } finally {
            if (tenancy()->initialized) {
                tenancy()->end();
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Files under database/migrations/tenant that the CURRENT (initialized)
     * tenant database has not recorded.
     *
     * @return list<string>
     */
    private function pendingTenantMigrations(): array
    {
        $path = database_path('migrations/tenant');
        if (! File::isDirectory($path)) {
            return [];
        }

        $onDisk = [];
        foreach (File::files($path) as $file) {
            if ($file->getExtension() === 'php') {
                $onDisk[] = $file->getFilenameWithoutExtension();
            }
        }
        sort($onDisk);

        /** @var list<string> $ran */
        $ran = DB::table('migrations')->pluck('migration')->all();

        return array_values(array_diff($onDisk, $ran));
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 3 — the two helpers this test needs, added to `ProvisionsTenantDatabases` in full** (plan gate r1 **M0b-1**). Rev 1 called `provisionTenants()` and `dropTenantDatabase()` and left their implementation to prose; **neither exists.** The trait's actual API is `provisionTenantDatabase(Tenant): Tenant`, `provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema(Tenant): Tenant` and `withinTenantDatabase(Tenant, callable): mixed` (`apps/api/tests/Traits/ProvisionsTenantDatabases.php:35,86,130`). Both helpers are written **in terms of those**, in the trait's own style, and both arms (PG and SQLite) mirror what `provisionTenantDatabase()` already does. Append them to the trait:

~~~php
    /**
     * N tenants, each with a real per-tenant database carrying the full schema.
     *
     * TenantFleetRunner's readiness predicate reads the tenant's `migrations`
     * table, so an EMPTY database is not enough: provisionTenantDatabase() alone
     * would make every tenant `tenant_not_ready:migrations_behind` and the
     * selector cases could never reach their closure. Hence
     * provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema().
     *
     * @return list<Tenant>
     */
    protected function provisionTenants(int $count): array
    {
        $tenants = [];

        for ($i = 0; $i < $count; $i++) {
            $slug = 'fleet-'.strtolower(Str::random(12));
            $tenant = Tenant::create([
                'name' => 'Fleet '.$slug,
                'slug' => $slug,
                'status' => TenantStatus::Active,
                'plan' => SubscriptionPlan::Trial,
                'country_code' => 'FR',
                'currency_code' => 'EUR',
            ]);

            $tenants[] = $this->provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema($tenant);
        }

        return $tenants;
    }

    /**
     * Delete a tenant's DATABASE while LEAVING its directory row — the
     * "directory row with no database" case TenancyResolver probes for, and the
     * one TenantFleetRunner must report as FAILED rather than skip.
     *
     * The connection is purged first: a cached `tenant` connection to a database
     * that no longer exists makes the NEXT initialize() throw a driver error
     * instead of returning false from databaseExists().
     */
    protected function dropTenantDatabase(Tenant $tenant): void
    {
        if (tenancy()->initialized) {
            tenancy()->end();
        }
        DB::purge('tenant');

        if (DB::connection()->getDriverName() === 'pgsql') {
            $tenant->database()->manager()->deleteDatabase($tenant);

            return;
        }

        $path = database_path($tenant->getDatabaseName());
        if (is_file($path)) {
            unlink($path);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Make a provisioned tenant look MIGRATIONS-BEHIND without touching the
     * migration files: delete the last recorded row from its `migrations` table,
     * so exactly one on-disk file is unaccounted for.
     *
     * @return string the migration name removed, for the assertion message
     */
    protected function makeTenantMigrationsBehind(Tenant $tenant): string
    {
        return $this->withinTenantDatabase($tenant, static function (): string {
            /** @var object{id: int, migration: string}|null $last */
            $last = DB::table('migrations')->orderByDesc('id')->first();
            if ($last === null) {
                throw new RuntimeException('tenant has no migrations recorded; provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema did not run.');
            }
            DB::table('migrations')->where('id', $last->id)->delete();

            return (string) $last->migration;
        });
    }
~~~

      New imports the trait needs: `App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\TenantStatus`, `App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\SubscriptionPlan`, `Illuminate\Support\Str`, `RuntimeException`. **Check each against the trait's existing import list before adding it** (`DB` and `Tenant` are already there at `:11,10`), and name the three additions in the handback per this plan's File Structure note.

      > **On the SQLite arm, `provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema()` clones DDL from `sqlite_master` and copies no rows** (`ProvisionsTenantDatabases.php:86-117`), so the cloned `migrations` table is **empty**. That is fine for `makeTenantMigrationsBehind()` only on the **PG** arm, where `MigrateDatabase` really runs and really records rows (`:90-94`). `TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest` is a **PG-only** test for exactly this reason — see Step 5.

- [ ] **Step 4 — the selector test (EC-39), parameterised over the fleet commands.** In wave 0b only `permissions:ensure-fleet` exists; wave 1 extends the same class to `permissions:sync-fleet`. **The data provider is written now, with one real row, so wave 1 adds a row rather than a file** (plan gate r1 **M0b-1**; wave 1's plan promises to "extend" it). The provider's row is *(command name, the minimum arguments that make it runnable, the marker prefix it renders)*, and the command-level case asserts the two properties a fleet command must share regardless of what its closure does: **an unmatched selector exits non-zero**, and **the aggregate line names the selector**. The runner-level cases stay direct instantiations, because the selector contract is the runner's.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Tenant;

use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs\FleetTenantOutcome;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\Services\TenantFleetRunner;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\DataProvider;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\TestCase;
use Tests\Traits\ProvisionsTenantDatabases;

/**
 * EC-39 — the step-1a selector contract (spec 4.3.3's rev-9 M-2 ruling).
 *
 * The contrast that motivates it: RollingTenantMigrationCommand accepts a
 * single --tenant UUID, resolves it with an exact where('id', …), only WARNS
 * when it matches nothing, and then treats an empty tenant set as SUCCESS
 * (apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Commands/RollingTenantMigrationCommand.php:143-146,61-70).
 * Copying that shape would make a TYPO in the remediation command this
 * programme prescribes attempt zero tenants, print "no tenants", exit 0, and
 * let the operator write permissions_ensure=ok over a fleet never repaired.
 *
 * GATE r9 m-1 IS APPLIED: the ambiguous case is a CROSS-COLUMN collision — a
 * selector equal to tenant A's UUID and tenant B's slug — never two equal
 * slugs, because tenants.slug is globally unique
 * (apps/api/database/migrations/2025_11_30_000001_create_tenants_table.php:19).
 * The snapshot primitive is get(['id','slug']); pluck('id','slug') would
 * collapse duplicate keys and could not represent a match set at all.
 */
final class TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest extends TestCase
{
    use ProvisionsTenantDatabases;

    private TenantFleetRunner $runner;

    protected function setUp(): void
    {
        parent::setUp();
        $this->runner = new TenantFleetRunner;
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_empty_selector_list_is_the_whole_snapshot(): void
    {
        $tenants = $this->provisionTenants(2);

        $result = $this->runner->run([], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertSame(2, $result->tenants());
        $this->assertTrue($result->isGreen());
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_tenant_resolves_by_id_and_by_slug(): void
    {
        [$a, $b] = $this->provisionTenants(2);

        $byId = $this->runner->run([(string) $a->id], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));
        $bySlug = $this->runner->run([(string) $b->slug], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertSame([(string) $a->id], array_map(static fn (FleetTenantOutcome $o): string => $o->subject, $byId->outcomes));
        $this->assertSame([(string) $b->id], array_map(static fn (FleetTenantOutcome $o): string => $o->subject, $bySlug->outcomes));
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_id_and_the_slug_of_the_same_tenant_collapse_to_one(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $processed = 0;

        $result = $this->runner->run(
            [(string) $a->id, (string) $a->slug],
            'TEST',
            function (Tenant $t) use (&$processed): FleetTenantOutcome {
                $processed++;

                return FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id);
            },
        );

        $this->assertSame(1, $processed, 'duplication is an operator convenience, not an error — and double-processing would make applied= disagree with the fleet size.');
        $this->assertSame(1, $result->tenants());
        $this->assertTrue($result->isGreen());
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_unmatched_selector_fails_by_its_literal_string_and_does_not_abandon_the_rest(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $processed = [];

        $result = $this->runner->run(
            ['no-such-tenant', (string) $a->id],
            'TEST',
            function (Tenant $t) use (&$processed): FleetTenantOutcome {
                $processed[] = (string) $t->id;

                return FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id);
            },
        );

        $this->assertSame([(string) $a->id], $processed, 'per-selector isolation: one typo must not abandon the rest of a remediation batch.');
        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen());
        $line = $result->aggregateLine('TEST');
        $this->assertStringContainsString('failed=1', $line);
        $this->assertStringContainsString('no-such-tenant', $line);
        $this->assertStringContainsString('tenant_selector_unmatched', $line);
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_cross_column_collision_is_ambiguous_and_names_both_ids(): void
    {
        [$a, $b] = $this->provisionTenants(2);
        // The reachable ambiguity: tenant B's SLUG is set to tenant A's UUID.
        // slug is globally unique, so this is legal; two equal slugs are not.
        $b->forceFill(['slug' => (string) $a->id])->save();

        $result = $this->runner->run([(string) $a->id], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen());
        $line = $result->aggregateLine('TEST');
        $this->assertStringContainsString('tenant_selector_ambiguous', $line);
        $this->assertStringContainsString((string) $a->id, $line);
        $this->assertStringContainsString((string) $b->id, $line);
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_unmatched_selector_does_not_exit_zero_the_way_rolling_migration_does(): void
    {
        $this->provisionTenants(1);

        $result = $this->runner->run(['typo-in-the-runbook'], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertSame(0, $result->count(\App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\FleetOutcome::Applied));
        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen(), 'a zero-match run must NOT be green — that is the RollingTenantMigrationCommand shape this contract deliberately rejects.');
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_tenant_whose_database_is_absent_is_failed_not_skipped(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $this->dropTenantDatabase($a);

        $result = $this->runner->run([], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen());
        $this->assertStringContainsString('tenant_not_ready:database_absent', $result->aggregateLine('TEST'));
    }

    /**
     * The OTHER half of the readiness predicate, and the one that actually
     * happens in production: the entrypoint runs `tenants:migrate-rolling
     * --force` unconditionally and CATCHES its failure
     * (apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:150-154), so an established tenant sitting
     * one migration behind is routine. Rev 1 of this plan tested only the absent
     * database and left this arm — half the predicate — unexercised
     * (plan gate r1 M0b-1).
     */
    #[Test]
    public function a_tenant_whose_migrations_are_behind_is_failed_not_skipped(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $removed = $this->makeTenantMigrationsBehind($a);
        $reached = false;

        $result = $this->runner->run([], 'TEST', function (Tenant $t) use (&$reached): FleetTenantOutcome {
            $reached = true;

            return FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id);
        });

        $this->assertFalse($reached, "the closure must not run for a tenant missing '{$removed}': a fleet run that cannot prove it synced a tenant must not report success for it.");
        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen());
        $this->assertStringContainsString('tenant_not_ready:migrations_behind:1', $result->aggregateLine('TEST'));
    }

    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
    // THE TWO MEMBERS BELOW SHIP IN TASK 4'S COMMIT, NOT THIS ONE.
    // They invoke `permissions:ensure-fleet`, which Task 4 creates; committing
    // them here would be a knowingly red commit (Global Constraints, plan gate
    // r1 B0b-2). They are written out here because this is the class that owns
    // them and the class wave 1 extends. Task 4 Step 11 adds them and
    // re-stages this file.
    // ------------------------------------------------------------------

    /**
     * Wave 1 ADDS A ROW HERE for permissions:sync-fleet. The two properties are
     * the ones every fleet command shares no matter what its closure does, and
     * they are exactly the two RollingTenantMigrationCommand gets wrong.
     *
     * @return array<string, array{0: string, 1: array<string, mixed>, 2: string}>
     */
    public static function fleetCommands(): array
    {
        return [
            'permissions:ensure-fleet' => [
                'permissions:ensure-fleet',
                ['--keys' => ['products.view'], '--dry-run' => true],
                'PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-FLEET',
            ],
            // wave 1 appends:
            // 'permissions:sync-fleet' => ['permissions:sync-fleet', ['--dry-run' => true], 'PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET'],
        ];
    }

    /**
     * @param  array<string, mixed>  $arguments
     */
    #[Test]
    #[DataProvider('fleetCommands')]
    public function every_fleet_command_exits_non_zero_on_an_unmatched_selector(string $command, array $arguments, string $markerPrefix): void
    {
        $this->provisionTenants(1);

        $exitCode = $this->artisan($command, [...$arguments, '--tenants' => ['typo-in-the-runbook']])
            ->expectsOutputToContain('tenant_selector_unmatched')
            ->expectsOutputToContain($markerPrefix)
            ->run();

        $this->assertSame(
            1,
            $exitCode,
            "{$command} exited 0 on a selector that matched nothing. That is the RollingTenantMigrationCommand shape this contract rejects: an operator would write permissions_ensure=ok over a fleet never repaired.",
        );
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_tenant_provisioned_after_the_start_snapshot_is_counted_as_a_population_gauge_only(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $late = null;

        $result = $this->runner->run([], 'TEST', function (Tenant $t) use (&$late): FleetTenantOutcome {
            $late ??= $this->provisionTenants(1)[0];

            return FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id);
        });

        $this->assertSame(1, $result->tenants(), 'a directory row that appears after the snapshot is not in this run\'s universe.');
        $this->assertSame(1, $result->provisionedDuringRun);
        $this->assertTrue($result->isGreen(), 'the gauge asserts NOTHING about that tenant\'s catalogue state and must never make the run non-green.');
    }
}
~~~

## Task 4 (0b-9c part 2 + 0b-14): `permissions:ensure`, `permissions:ensure-fleet`, and the lock wired into every runtime writer — **ONE commit**

> **This is one commit by spec ruling, not by preference.** §8's 0b-14 row: *"WIRING that lock into every runtime role-grant writer that exists at 0b, **in the same lane and the same commit as `EnsurePermissions.php`**"*. The 0b-window argument (§4.3.2, rev-7 B-1) is why: with every runtime writer serialized on the one advisory key, `ensure`'s post-lock eligibility check is decisive on its own — any operator change to a `--grant-to` role either commits **before** `ensure` acquires the lock (the post-lock re-read finds the role no longer equal to `postA1aGrants`, `matchesPreWave0b()` is false, and the role falls into `admin_only=`) or commits **after** `ensure` releases (and lands on top of a grant set `ensure` had already decided was eligible). There is no third interleaving **because there is no unlocked writer left to produce one**. Split the two and there is: for the whole 0b window `ensure` would be serialized against nothing, and the equality check would be a race with no detector.

### Why `permissions:ensure` exists at all

After `lane/w-lot-a-1a` merges, `php artisan tenants:seed --class=…RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` **writes nothing** on a marked tenant while `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE` is false — its shipped default. `run()` falls past the enforcement branch, hits `markedTenantCarriesWlota1aDelta()`, emits its `WLOTA1A-RESEED … marked_tenant_delta_preserved` marker and **returns** (lane `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:52-55`, predicate at `:72-78`). That is *correct* for the lane, whose whole point is that a marked tenant is never re-seeded. The consequence for 0b is fatal: the nineteen keys would never reach a marked tenant while its routes are already gated on them, and Spatie fails closed — **403 for everyone, including `admin`**. That is the `credit-notes.cancel` failure mode reproduced on nineteen fresh keys.

### The contract, deliberately the smallest thing that closes the hole

- **Creates only, through an explicit existence check.** Never renames, never deprecates, never deletes, never revokes. The implementation is `exists()` **then** `Permission::create(...)` rather than `Permission::firstOrCreate(...)`, and rev 2 says so in both places (rev 1's prose said `firstOrCreate` while its code did `exists()`+`create()` — plan gate r1 minor). The reason is not style: the runner must **count** creations (`created=`) and must be able to count them in a `--dry-run` where nothing is written, and `firstOrCreate` gives neither — it returns a model without telling you which branch it took, and it writes on the dry-run path. Under the advisory lock the two-statement form has no race the one-statement form does not.
- **Grants to `admin` unconditionally — and a tenant with no resolvable `admin` is a FAILURE.** The registry-era rule is `admin = activeKeys()` and `admin` is not operator-editable at all, so granting `admin` a new key restores an invariant rather than widening a choice. **The converse is the hard part** (plan gate r1 **B0b-5**): if the `admin` role cannot be resolved, the tenant has **nobody** holding the keys its routes are now gated on — Spatie fails closed, so that tenant is 403 for every caller on twenty-five write routes. Rev 1 appended `'admin'` to `missingRoles`, continued, and computed the outcome from `created`/`granted` alone, so such a tenant could emit `APPLIED` or `ALREADY_CURRENT`, exit **zero**, and be marked deploy-safe. Rev 2: a missing or unresolvable `admin` is **`FAILED` with `reason=admin_role_missing`**, non-zero exit, on **both** the applying and the `--dry-run` path — a dry run is write-free, not failure-free.
- **Grants a named `--grant-to` role only when `matchesPreWave0b()` is true**, evaluated **once per role, from the post-lock re-read, before any of that role's additions are written**, and the approved additions are then written as **one batch**. Evaluating per key would be the same defect at a smaller scale: the first granted key makes the role unequal and every subsequent key in the same invocation would be refused.
- **Otherwise: `admin` only, and say so.** The role is skipped, listed in `admin_only=`, and printed with the operator sentence. **A skip is not a failure** — the run still exits 0, because a tenant that customised its `manager` and therefore does not receive a new key is the correct outcome, not an incident.
- **Writes no marker.** It touches neither `provisioning_source` nor `general_manager`'s existence, which is exactly why it is safe where `tenants:seed` deliberately no-ops.
- **Team-bounded and NULL-team-tolerant.** `SetPermissionsTeam` is HTTP-only (`apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Middleware/SetPermissionsTeam.php:22-29`), and a console command has nothing to supply the team from. `ensure` therefore takes the identical boundary the lane ships — set before the transaction, restored in a `finally` on **every** path including failure — and resolves every role with the lane's NULL-or-tenant predicate. After the lane merges the legacy catalogue roles stay **NULL-team** while `general_manager` is **tenant-team-scoped**, so a lookup that ignores the team column would either miss `general_manager` entirely or resolve a different row than the `FOR UPDATE` that locked it — and a lock over one topology guarding a write over another is not serialization.

### Steps — part A: the runner

- [ ] **Step 1 — the outcome enum and the result DTO.**

~~~php

{
    case Applied = 'APPLIED';
~~~
