- [ ] **Step 4 — the catalogue assertion, in the commit that completes the catalogue.** Create `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/Wave0bAdditionCatalogueTest.php` — the half deliberately kept out of Task 1 so no commit was knowingly red (plan gate r1 **B0b-2**):

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Identity;

use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\LegacyRoleBaseline;
use Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\TestCase;

/**
 * The constant and the CATALOGUE agree. Ships with Task 6, the commit that
 * declares the last of the nineteen keys — an undeclared key is a silent 403 for
 * every caller including admin, because Spatie 6.25 swallows
 * PermissionDoesNotExist in checkPermissionTo.
 */
final class Wave0bAdditionCatalogueTest extends TestCase
{
    #[Test]
    public function every_wave_0b_addition_is_declared_in_the_seeder(): void
    {
        $declared = RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::permissionNames();

        foreach (LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions('admin') as $key) {
            $this->assertContains(
                $key,
                $declared,
                "key '{$key}' is in WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS and gates a live route, but is not in the catalogue — "
                .'Spatie fails closed and every caller including admin gets 403.',
            );
        }
    }

    #[Test]
    public function the_catalogue_grew_by_exactly_nineteen(): void
    {
        $this->assertCount(19, LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions('admin'));
    }

    #[Test]
    public function the_seven_grant_to_keys_reach_manager_and_general_manager_in_the_seeder(): void
    {
        $grants = RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::rolePermissionGrants();

        foreach (['manager', 'general_manager'] as $role) {
            foreach (LegacyRoleBaseline::wave0bAdditions('manager') as $key) {
                $this->assertContains($key, $grants[$role], "the seeder does not give {$role} '{$key}', but the deploy's --grant-to does. A NEW tenant would differ from a migrated one.");
            }
        }
    }
}

## Task 7 (0b-5): gate the two BatchExpiry writes **beside** `BatchActionAccess`

**The lane already attaches middleware to both routes** — `BatchActionAccess::class.':batches.delete'` at lane `apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php:27` and `:batches.recall` at `:30`. **That is not coverage.** The middleware returns `$next($request)` with **no permission check at all** while `LotActionPermissionActivation::enforced()` is false (`lane/…/Presentation/Middleware/BatchActionAccess.php:18-20`), and `false` is the shipped default (`lane/…/config/lot_action_permissions.php:5`). Counting it as a gate would mark two live writes as gated on every tenant that has not flipped the flag.

**0b-5 adds a plain `can:` beside it, never instead of it.** The route then gates unconditionally, and the lane's flag keeps governing only the lane's own stricter behaviour.

**Files:** `apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php` (lane `:27,30`), the ratchet test, the baseline.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — both keys already exist.** `batches.delete` and `batches.recall` are in the seeded catalogue; the audit called them "dead" **precisely because** these two routes had no gate. `batches.recall` is additionally re-granted by the lane to `general_manager` and revoked from `manager`. **Neither key is declared or moved by this wave**, and neither may be deprecated in wave 1 — §4.9.3's four exclusions.

- [ ] **Step 2 — stack the gates:**
      ```php
      Route::delete('/batches/{uuid}', [BatchController::class, 'destroy'])
          ->middleware([BatchActionAccess::class.':batches.delete', 'can:batches.delete']);

      Route::post('/batches/{uuid}/recall', [BatchController::class, 'recall'])
          ->middleware([BatchActionAccess::class.':batches.recall', 'can:batches.recall']);
      ```
      Stacked middleware is an **AND** by construction; nothing new is needed.

- [ ] **Step 3 — the classifier must still refuse the flag-conditional middleware.** `RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest::a_flag_conditional_middleware_is_not_gating` (wave 0a) pins `BatchActionAccess` as the worked example. It must **stay green after this change** — the route is now gated by the `can:` half, and the assertion is about the *classifier's alias list*, not about the route. If it goes red, the classifier has started accepting `BatchActionAccess`, which is the defect it exists to prevent.

- [ ] **Step 4 — delete the two baseline entries, lower `UNCOVERED_WRITE_CEILING` by 2, run the batch suite, commit.**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w0b/apps/api && ./vendor/bin/phpunit tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php tests/Feature/BatchExpiry
      ```
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php apps/api/tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/route-permission-coverage-baseline.json
      ```
      ```
      Phase 0.2.7: Gate the batch delete and recall writes BESIDE BatchActionAccess

      lane/w-lot-a-1a attaches BatchActionAccess to both routes, but that middleware
      returns $next() with no check at all while LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE is
      false — its shipped default — so it is not coverage, and the ratchet's
      classifier already pins it as the worked example of a flag-conditional
      middleware that is not a gate. A plain can:batches.delete / can:batches.recall
      is stacked BESIDE it (stacked middleware is an AND), so the routes are gated
      unconditionally while the lane's flag keeps governing only the lane's own
      stricter behaviour.

      Neither key is declared or moved here — both are already seeded, and both are
      among the four keys §4.9.3 excludes from deprecation for exactly this reason.

      Location scope on these two writes is a SEPARATE, filed gap: neither consults
      LocationScopeResolver. Ticket
      docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-batch-writes-ignore-location-scope.md,
      wave 3.

Its only production caller is manual (`ProductionSeeder`, never in the entrypoint or the provisioning path).

| `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/GuardConsistencyTest.php:17,113-129` | imports it; asserts *"PermissionSeeder created permissions/roles with the correct guard"* | **DELETED with its subject.** The guard invariant it asserts is re-asserted for the **real** catalogue by `EnsurePermissionsCustomisationGuardTest` (every created row is `guard_name = 'sanctum'`) and, in wave 1, by `PermissionsSyncTest`. Expect a `-N lines` diff, not a regression. |

> **The five re-points are the real work.** Each currently receives `PermissionSeeder`'s **capitalised** role names; re-pointing them at the real seeder gives them the lowercase seeded roles, so **every assertion that references a role by name must be read and adjusted in the same commit**. ## Task 12 (0b-11): the CI `env:` line that switches on the anti-growth direction

This task discharges wave 0a's residual **R-3**. It is a `.github/workflows/ci.yml` edit, and that file is edited by **both** in-flight lanes, which is why it waited for 0b's entry condition.

**R-3 — the anti-growth direction is inert.** `RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest`'s anti-growth case is a `markTestSkipped` until `ROUTE_COVERAGE_BASELINE_PROTECTED_BLOB` is registered. Until then a contributor can add a violation **and** its baseline entry in one commit — the loophole wave 0a's `RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetLivenessTest::matched_growth_defeats_the_checker_which_is_what_the_protected_blob_exists_for` documents executably.

> **R-4 IS ALREADY CLOSED — do not re-do it (wave 0a rev 2, plan gate r1 B-2).** Rev 1 of this plan said the new Architecture classes ran on no CI event and that registering them rode here. Both halves changed: the claim was wrong (they **did** run on `workflow_dispatch`, via the manual full-suite step at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:473-501`; what they did not do was gate PR→`dev`), and wave 0a now registers them itself — **Task 6 step 1 of the 0a plan appends a `backend-architecture` step after `:228` naming all six classes.** This task therefore adds **one `env:` block to that existing step**, not a new step. **Read the step that is actually in the file before editing**; if it is absent, wave 0a did not land its Task 6 step 1 and that is a finding, not a licence to re-create it here with a different class list.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — add the `env:` block to wave 0a's existing step, appended, never a reflow.** Both lanes have hunks elsewhere in this file (W-LOT around `:1130`, T2's PG `--filter` allow-list around `:1138-1145`), so an **append** merges cleanly and a reformat does not. Locate the step wave 0a added after `:228` — `- name: RBAC route-coverage ratchet + self-service + tombstones (wave 0a)` — and give it an `env:` block, mirroring the `backend-dpa-guard` shape at `:313-323`:
      ```yaml
      - name: RBAC route-coverage ratchet + self-service + tombstones (wave 0a)
        env:
          # OWNER-SET repository variable — the anti-growth ceiling, deliberately
          # outside every candidate diff. Unset => the ratchet fails closed, which
          # is the designed forcing function for the owner bootstrap. Same contract
          # as DPA_BASELINE_PROTECTED_BLOB above.
          ROUTE_COVERAGE_BASELINE_PROTECTED_BLOB: ${{ vars.ROUTE_COVERAGE_BASELINE_PROTECTED_BLOB }}
        run: |
          # …the six-class run wave 0a wrote, UNCHANGED. Do not re-list the
          # classes here: if this task and wave 0a disagree about which classes
          # run, the one that gates merges is whichever edit landed last.
      ```
      **Change nothing else in the step**, and add nothing to its `run:` block — the class list is wave 0a's and the zero-selection guard (the house pattern from `:305-311`) is already in it.
- [ ] **Step 2 — replace the ratchet's anti-growth `markTestSkipped`** with the fail-closed-on-unset form `DocumentPerActionBaselineRatchetTest` uses: an unset variable **fails** the test with the bootstrap instruction, rather than skipping.
- [ ] **Step 3 — the repository variable is the OWNER's.** Compute the blob and record it in the handback as an owner-owed item; the test fails closed until the owner sets it, which is the designed forcing function:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w0b/apps/api && git hash-object tests/Architecture/baselines/route-permission-coverage-baseline.json
      ```
- [ ] **Step 4 — validate the workflow before committing.** A YAML error in `ci.yml` takes the whole repository's CI down:
      ```

## Task 13: the convention-09 rows

Three tests, all asserting **data meaning** — the rows company B sees, the rows written at location B — never HTTP status alone (`docs/conventions/09-SECOND-OF-EVERYTHING.md:52`).

- [ ] **`ServicePermissionsSecondCompanyTest`** — an actor holding `services.update` in company A is refused `PATCH /services/{id}` on a service belonging to company B. Assert the **service row is unchanged**, not merely that the response was 403. Two companies in one tenant, created through the **real registration/company-create path**, not by direct model insert.
- [ ] **`CategoryPermissionsSecondCompanyTest`** — categories are **company**-scoped with no location binding at all (every read and write goes through `companyContext->requireCompanyId()` and a `where('company_id', …)`, `apps/api/app/Modules/Product/Presentation/Controllers/CategoryController.php:28-35,101-108,115-178`), so the axis they actually have is the company one. Assert the **rows returned** per company and that a create in company A does not appear in company B's tree.
- [ ] **`StockAdjustmentLocationScopePermissionTest`** — the second-location axis, on `POST /api/v1/stock-adjustments`. The gate is at `apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php:126-128` (`can:inventory.adjustments.create`) and the **location denial** is enforced at `apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/Requests/StoreStockAdjustmentRequest.php:90` (`new ValidLocationAccess($this->locationContext, $this->companyContext, $company->id)`). Assert that a principal **holding** the permission but restricted by `allowed_location_ids` to location A is refused at location B, and that **removing the restriction lets the identical payload through** — asserting the `stock_adjustments` rows both times.

      > **A prerequisite fact, recorded rather than worked around:** the `ValidateLocationAccess` middleware is registered as `validate.location.access` (`apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:118`) and is attached to **zero routes** in the tree. "A route that enforces the middleware" does not exist to point at; the location enforcement that **is** real on an inventory write is the `ValidLocationAccess` rule and the `LocationContext` guard. A test whose denial never fires proves nothing, which is why this test targets a route where the denial is real.

**The shape all three share, written out once so none of them is a prose stub** (plan gate r1 **M0b-3**). The body below is `ServicePermissionsSecondCompanyTest`'s; the other two follow it beat for beat, with the substitutions in the table underneath. Each is a `Tests\Feature\<Module>` class using `RefreshDatabase` and seeding `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` (never a fake payload, never a hand-built permission), and each has the same four beats:

~~~php
    #[Test]
    public function an_actor_scoped_to_a_is_refused_the_same_write_in_b(): void
    {
        // 1. FIXTURE — two of the thing. The second company is created through the
        //    REAL registration/company-create path (POST /api/v1/companies as an
        //    actor holding companies.create), never a direct Company::create().
        //    Convention 09's whole point is that a direct insert skips the code
        //    that would have gone wrong.
        [$companyA, $companyB] = $this->registerTwoCompanies();
        $actor = $this->userHolding(['services.update'], $companyA);

        // 2. THE ROW BEFORE. Capture the exact column the write would change.
        $service = $this->serviceIn($companyB, ['name' => 'B original']);

        // 3. THE ATTEMPT.
        $response = $this->actingAs($actor)->patchJson("/api/v1/services/{$service->id}", ['name' => 'A rewrote it']);

        // 4. THE ROW AFTER — the assertion that matters. Status alone would pass
        //    against a route that 500s, a route that silently no-ops, and a route
        //    that wrote to the wrong company.
        $this->assertDatabaseHas('services', ['id' => $service->id, 'name' => 'B original']);
        $response->assertForbidden();
    }
~~~

Per test, the specifics:

| Test | Fixture's second thing | The row asserted |
|---|---|---|
| `ServicePermissionsSecondCompanyTest` | a second **company**, via `POST /api/v1/companies` | the `services` row's `name` in company B is unchanged; and the positive half — the same actor **can** PATCH a company-A service, so the refusal is the company scope and not the new gate |
| `CategoryPermissionsSecondCompanyTest` | a second **company** | `GET /api/v1/categories/tree` for company A returns **only** company A's rows, and a `POST /api/v1/categories` in A produces **no** row visible in B's tree — asserted on the returned ids, not the count |
| `StockAdjustmentLocationScopePermissionTest` | a second **location** in one company | with `allowed_location_ids = [A]`, `POST /api/v1/stock-adjustments` at location B writes **no** `stock_adjustments` row; with the restriction removed, the **identical payload** writes exactly one. Both directions, both asserted on rows |

## Task 13b (0b-15): the four Identity read-route gates, and the Users/Roles page guards that must land with them

> **Provenance.** Wave 0a's **T3** (the four Identity read-route gates) and **T3b** (the `UsersPage` roles-query guard) were moved here by orchestrator ruling on plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B-3** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-plan-0a-codex-gate-r1.md`). The ruling calls this task **"0b-4 read-route gates + Users/Roles page guards"**; `0b-4` is already the accepted spec's id for the `categories.*` key block (plan Task 6, spec §8), so it ships as **0b-15** — the name and the content are the ruling's, only the identifier is disambiguated so no two items share one.

**Why it is here and not in 0a.** Gating `GET /roles` and `GET /permissions` breaks a page a `manager` can open. `RolesPage` fires both reads unconditionally (`apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.tsx:73-80,82-89`), its route is gated only on `moduleKey="settings"` → `settings.view` (`apps/web/src/routes/index.tsx:2344-2351`, `apps/web/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts:97`), and its Settings card carries no permission check at all — `SettingsSection` has no permission field (`apps/web/src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx:23-53`). `manager` holds `settings.view` (`apps/web/src/hooks/permissionsMap.generated.ts:254`) but neither `roles.view` nor `users.assign-roles`, both `admin`-only (`:243,272`; seeder `apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:379,382`). Gating the API without gating the page ships a guaranteed double 403 on load and the loading-error surface (`RolesPage.tsx:246-249`). The fix needs `RolesPage.tsx`, `routes/index.tsx` and `usePermissions.ts` — **all three in `lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s diff** — which is exactly what wave 0b's entry condition already waits for. Sequencing cost: zero.

**Today's defect this closes (I-18).** `GET /api/v1/users/{userId}/roles` has **no gate at all** and returns **any tenant user's full `getAllPermissions()` list to any authenticated caller** (`RoleController::userRoles`, payload at `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php:429-437`). `GET /roles`, `GET /roles/{id}` and `GET /permissions` are equally ungated, while the comments above them at `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:58` (*"Role management (requires roles.view or roles.manage permission)"*) and `:77` (*"User role assignment (requires users.assign-roles permission)"*) are false statements. This task makes them true.

**Scope boundary — gating and page guards now, PAYLOAD SHAPING in wave 2a.** Owner ruling D4 gives a bare `users.assign-roles` holder role **names**, not the permission matrix, and spec §4.6.4 specifies three payload shapes for it. That shaping changes `RoleController`'s response builders and is a **wave-2a** deliverable (spec §8). This task closes the *"any authenticated caller"* half and leaves the *"an assign-roles holder still sees the matrix"* half open — residual **R-0b-8** below.

**Files**

- Modify: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:58-64,77-78` — the four read gates.
- Modify: `apps/api/tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php` — add `IDENTITY_READ_CLOSURES` + its assertion; `UNCOVERED_READ_CEILING` **146 → 142**.
- Modify: `apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/route-permission-coverage-baseline.json` — delete four entries (298 → 294 before this wave's own closures).
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/settings/UsersPage.tsx:136` — gate the roles query's `enabled` on `users.assign-roles`.
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/settings/__tests__/UsersPage.tenantScope.test.tsx:85` — the fixture user gains `admin` so the roles-key assertion at `:175` still exercises an **enabled** query.
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/settings/UsersPage.test.tsx` — two guard cases.
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.tsx:73-89` — both queries gated on `roles.view`.
- Modify: `apps/web/src/routes/index.tsx` — the `roles` route gains `permission="roles.view"` beside its `moduleKey="settings"`. `dev:2343-2352` (guard `:2346`); **post-lane-merge `:2350-2359` (guard `:2353`)** — the number an executing agent will actually see.
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx:23-53,64-75` — `SettingsSection` gains an optional `permission`, the Roles card declares `roles.view`, and the grid filters on it.
- Create: `apps/web/e2e/rbac-role-read-gates.spec.ts` — the Playwright spec `AGENTS.md:12-16` requires for UI work.
- Modify: `apps/web/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts` — **expected UNTOUCHED.** It is listed because the ruling names it and because `lane/w-lot-a-1a` edits it: if the Roles card or route turns out to need a `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` entry, that is where it goes, and the overlap must be re-checked at that moment. `roles.view` is already in the generated map (`permissionsMap.generated.ts:243`), so `hasPermission('roles.view')` needs no change here.

**Interfaces**

- Consumes `RouteCoverageClassifier`, `RouteCoverage` and `RoutePermissionCoverageScanner` from wave 0a Task 2.
- Produces `RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest::IDENTITY_READ_CLOSURES: array<string, string>` — route key ⇒ the gate expression it must carry.
- No new permission key, no seeder edit, no migration. Every key already exists.

**The two keys are not interchangeable (owner ruling D4; restated at wave-0a plan gate r2 M-5).** Read this before writing any guard in this task:

| Surface | Guard key | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `UsersPage`'s `/roles` query (`UsersPage.tsx:136`) | **`users.assign-roles`** | The page's job is assigning roles. `GET /roles` deliberately accepts `roles.view` **or** `users.assign-roles` (spec `:1610,1616-1622`), so guarding this query on `roles.view` would hide the role-name list from exactly the actor the any-of gate exists for. |
| Both `RolesPage` queries, the `/settings/roles` route, the Settings Roles card | **`roles.view`** | These surface the permission **matrix**, which is what `roles.view` names. `GET /permissions` and `GET /roles/{id}` are `can:roles.view` only. |

**Gate mapping (spec §4.6.4, owner ruling D4).** Both keys are in `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::permissionNames()` at `:379` (`users.assign-roles`) and `:382` (`roles.view`) — **not** `:382-383`; `:383` is `roles.manage` (gate r2 citation audit).

| Route | Line at base | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/roles` | `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:59` | `require.any.permission:roles.view,users.assign-roles` — a role **assigner** must be able to list role names |
| `GET /api/v1/roles/{id}` | `:61` | `can:roles.view` — the detail carries the permission set |
| `GET /api/v1/permissions` | `:64` | `can:roles.view` |
| `GET /api/v1/users/{userId}/roles` | `:78` | `require.any.permission:roles.view,users.assign-roles` |

The **five** write routes in those blocks — `POST /roles` (`:60`), `PATCH /roles/{id}` (`:62`), `DELETE /roles/{id}` (`:63`), `POST` and `DELETE /users/{userId}/roles` (`:79,80`) — are **left alone**: they are checked in `RoleController` today and their conversion to route gates is wave 3's consistency sweep. (Rev 1 of the 0a plan said "three write routes" and then named five — gate r1 m-3.)

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the backend red.** In `apps/api/tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php`, add the constant and the test:

~~~php
    /**
     * 0b-15, spec 4.6.4 (owner ruling D4). Four reads that are ungated today; the
     * worst of them, GET users/{userId}/roles, returns any tenant user's full
     * getAllPermissions() list to ANY authenticated caller (I-18).
     *
     * Every key already exists in the seeded catalogue — this task adds none.
     *
     * PAYLOAD SHAPING IS WAVE 2a, NOT HERE: D4 gives a bare `users.assign-roles`
     * holder role NAMES, not the matrix, and that lives in RoleController's
     * response builders. This task closes the "any authenticated caller" half;
     * residual R-0b-8 carries the rest.
     *
     * @var array<string, string>
     */
    public const IDENTITY_READ_CLOSURES = [
        'GET api/v1/roles' => 'require.any.permission:roles.view,users.assign-roles',
        'GET api/v1/roles/{id}' => 'can:roles.view',
        'GET api/v1/permissions' => 'can:roles.view',
        'GET api/v1/users/{userId}/roles' => 'require.any.permission:roles.view,users.assign-roles',
    ];

    #[Test]
    public function the_identity_role_reads_carry_their_declared_gate(): void
    {
        $classifier = new RouteCoverageClassifier;

        foreach (self::IDENTITY_READ_CLOSURES as $key => $expected) {
            $route = $this->findRoute($key);

            self::assertSame(
                RouteCoverage::Gated,
                $classifier->classify($route),
                $key.' is not gated. Spec 4.6.4 requires '.$expected.'.',
            );
            self::assertContains(
                $expected,
                array_values(array_filter(
                    $route->gatherMiddleware(),
                    static fn (string|object $middleware): bool => is_string($middleware),
                )),
                $key.' is gated, but not with the declared expression '.$expected.'.',
            );
        }
    }

(`lane/w-lot-a-1a` `52f5ad796` **does** touch `routes/index.tsx`, `RolesPage.tsx` and `usePermissions.ts` — that is the entry condition this whole wave waits for, and by the time this task runs the lane is an ancestor of `dev`, so its edits are the base, not a conflict. The `routes/index.tsx` line numbers this task cites are **post-merge**: the lane shifts everything after `:347` by **+2** and everything after the batches block by **+7**, so `/settings/roles` is `:2350-2359` with its guard at `:2353`, not `:2343-2352`/`:2346`.)

- [ ] **Step 10 — commit** (one commit: the gates and the guards are one change, and splitting them is what created the defect).
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php apps/api/tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/route-permission-coverage-baseline.json apps/web/src/features/settings/UsersPage.tsx apps/web/src/features/settings/UsersPage.test.tsx apps/web/src/features/settings/__tests__/UsersPage.tenantScope.test.tsx apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.tsx apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.test.tsx apps/web/src/routes/index.tsx apps/web/src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx apps/web/e2e/rbac-role-read-gates.spec.ts
      ```
      ```
      Phase 0.2.15: Gate the four role/permission read routes and the pages that call them (I-18)

      GET /users/{userId}/roles returned ANY tenant user's full
      getAllPermissions() list to ANY authenticated caller (RoleController.php
      :429-437). GET /roles, GET /roles/{id} and GET /permissions were equally
      ungated, while the comments above them claimed a permission was required.

      Gates, per owner ruling D4 and spec 4.6.4, using only seeded keys:
        GET roles                  require.any.permission:roles.view,users.assign-roles
        GET roles/{id}             can:roles.view
        GET permissions            can:roles.view
        GET users/{userId}/roles   require.any.permission:roles.view,users.assign-roles

      The FRONTEND GUARDS SHIP IN THE SAME COMMIT, which is the whole reason this
      task is here rather than in wave 0a (plan gate r1, BLOCKER B-3). Neither key
      is granted to any seeded role but admin, and a `manager` (settings.view +
      users.view) reaches BOTH Settings -> Users and Settings -> Roles. Without
      the guards these gates would 403 on every manager page load: UsersPage fired
      GET /roles unconditionally, and RolesPage fires GET /roles AND
      GET /permissions with no permission check, behind a route gated only on
      moduleKey="settings" and a Settings card with no check at all.

        - UsersPage: the roles query is enabled only for users.assign-roles.
        - RolesPage: both queries are enabled only for roles.view.
        - The /settings/roles route gains permission="roles.view".
        - The Settings hub filters the Roles card on roles.view, so a card never
          leads to a page its viewer will be refused.

      Four baseline entries deleted in this commit — the ratchet's stale direction
      refuses a fix that forgets its entry — and UNCOVERED_READ_CEILING 146 -> 142,
      the four reads wave 0a deliberately did not claim.

      PAYLOAD SHAPING IS NOT HERE. D4 also says a bare users.assign-roles holder
      gets role NAMES, not the matrix; that lives in RoleController's response
      builders and is wave 2a. Residual R-0b-8 carries it.

      Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
      Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Bp1WuD39YqaMWiHR8vPDNN
      ```

      > **Commit-subject numbering.** `AGENTS.md:15-16` requires `Phase <major.minor.patch>: <imperative summary>`. Wave 0a uses `Phase 0.1.<task>`; wave 0b uses **`Phase 0.2.<task>`**, so this one is `Phase 0.2.15`. Apply the same scheme to the other 0b tasks when their commits are written; the bodies stay as each task states them.

## Reviewer gate

**`tenancy-authz-reviewer` — mandatory, on the whole diff.** Prompt outline:

> Review lane `lane/rbac-w0b` (base `dev` at `<sha>`, tip `<sha>`) against
> `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` rev 9
> §§4.3.2, 4.3.3, 4.3.5, 4.4.3, 4.4.4, 4.9.3, 4.9.4, 6 and 8's wave-0b rows, and
> against this plan. Cite `file:line` for every claim; verify against the tree,
> never from the diff alone.
>
> 1. **Is the lock ONE domain?** Confirm `PermissionWriteLock::key()` produces the
>    same string and the same hash function as `LotActionPermissionDelta`'s own
>    advisory lock. A different key is not a weaker lock — it is **no** lock
>    between the two writers. Then confirm every LOCKED writer orders its
>    `FOR UPDATE` by `roles.name` then `roles.id`, and say which pair of roles
>    would deadlock if one of them used `id` alone.
>  2. **Is the writer partition real, at METHOD level?** Re-run
>    `PermissionWriterCensus` yourself and print the full `<path>::<method>` set.
>    Name every method it enumerates that the three fixtures do not classify.
>    Then check the two exemption lists specifically: for each
>    `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM` entry, confirm from the AST that its declared entry
>    point acquires before calling it **and** that nothing else in the file calls
>    it; for each `INITIALIZATION_ONLY` entry, confirm it is genuinely
>    unreachable from an authenticated request. **A file-level answer is not an
>    answer** — the question is whether the METHOD acquires before its first
>    write. Add an unlocked method to `RoleController` and confirm the test goes
>    red naming it.
> 3. **Is `ensure`'s eligibility check decisive?** Trace the four statements
>    between `acquire()` and the first `givePermissionTo()`. Prove the grant set
>    it compares was read **after** the lock, and that the snapshot is taken once
>    per role and not once per key. Then construct the interleaving that would
>    lose an operator edit and show why it cannot occur.
> 4. **Does `ensure` resolve a tenant-team `general_manager` from the console?**
>    Open the resolution query. Confirm the NULL-or-tenant predicate, that
>    `tenant_id` is preserved as found, that no role is re-homed, and that the
>    registrar's team id is restored on the failure path as well as the success
>    path.
> 5. **Is every gated key actually seeded?** For each of the nineteen, find it in
>    `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::legacyPermissionNames()` and cite the line. Then
>    name, per key, which seeded roles hold it and which live UI surface calls the
>    route — an undeclared or unheld key is a silent 403 for everyone including
>    `admin`, which is the exact defect 0b-1 exists to fix.
> 6. **Is any live surface broken?** Enumerate every frontend and POS caller of
>    the forty-one gated routes and, for each, name the roles that can reach the
>    calling page and whether they hold the key. `services.*` is the known one —
>    verify the FE guard moved to `services.update` and that no second `services.edit`
>    reference survives.
> 7. **Is the ceiling arithmetic right?** Re-derive 127/126 independently from the
>    live router. Confirm no baseline entry is left stale and that the anti-growth
>    direction is now fail-closed rather than skipped.
> 8. **`BatchActionAccess`:** confirm the `can:` was added **beside** it, that the
>    classifier still refuses to count it as a gate, and that the flag's default
>    is unchanged.
> 9. **`ResetTenantCommand`:** confirm the lock is taken **after** the schema
>    recreate, that the existing `setPermissionsTeamId` at its original position
>    survives, and that the plan's out-of-scope statement about `DROP SCHEMA` is
>    still true of the code.
> 10. **Overlap:** re-run `git diff --name-only dev...lane/t2-receipt-spine -- <path>`
>     for every path in the diff (and the same for any lane opened since). Any hit
>     is a blocker.
> 10b. **0b-15 — do the gates and the guards actually match?** For each of the four gated
>    reads, name every `apps/web` caller and the roles that can reach its page, and confirm
>    the page's guard uses the SAME key the route does. `RolesPage` (both queries), the
>    `/settings/roles` route, the Settings card and `UsersPage`'s roles query are the four
>    surfaces; a fifth caller anywhere in `apps/web/src` or `apps/pos/src` is a blocker.
>    Then confirm the Playwright spec asserts the **absence** of the requests, not a 403 —
>    a page that fires a request it knows will be refused is the defect this task exists to
>    close, and a 403 in the network log means the guard is not doing its job.
> 11. **The four open questions Q-0b-1..Q-0b-4:** answer each, or say which need
>     the owner. Do not let them merge as discovered decisions.
>
> Verdict: MERGE / MERGE-WITH-FIXES / CHANGES-REQUIRED. Never auto-merge.

**`inventory-costing-reviewer` — mandatory**, for 0b-5 (a batch delete and a batch recall) and 0b-13 (a counting-item submit). Ask specifically: does gating the batch writes on permission change *when* stock moves, or only *who* may move it? Does `can:inventory.adjust` on the counting-item submit match the authority the rest of the counting flow assumes, or is `inventory.adjustments.create` the truer key? Is the filed location-scope gap (`docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-batch-writes-ignore-location-scope.md`) an accurate description of what the controller does?

**`frontend-conventions-reviewer` — mandatory, and a PRECONDITION for Task 6's and Task 13b's commits, not a review of them afterwards** (owner's standing rule: every FE milestone). The lane touches **sixteen** `apps/web` callers, six service-category call sites, `apps/web/src/routes/index.tsx`, `permissionsMap.generated.ts`, three locale bundles and two new Playwright specs. Ask specifically:
> 1. Is the `services.edit` → `services.update` guard change complete — no second `services.edit` reference anywhere in `apps/web` or `apps/pos`?
> 2. **Is every one of the sixteen callers in Task 6's census actually guarded**, and does each guard use the SAME key as the route it calls? A guard on a different key is worse than none, because it looks addressed.
> 3. Are **all six** `/services/categories` API call sites fixed — including `ServiceListPage.tsx:49`, which the gate register did not name — with `api.put` → `api.patch` on update, while the two SPA paths (`ServiceListPage.tsx:117`, `Sidebar.tsx:148`) and every `tenantScopedKey(['service-categories'])` are untouched?
> 4. Do the query keys still satisfy `audit-tanstack-keys.mjs`, and does `audit-design-system.mjs` stay green (the guards should add no markup and no colour literal)?
> 5. Does any new guard introduce a hand-rolled type beside a generated DTO, or a user-facing string outside `t()`?
> 6. Is the regenerated map byte-identical across two generations? Are the new label keys present in **all three** locales with real strings, or is French/Arabic owed and recorded as R-0b-1?
> 7. In both Playwright specs, does the manager arm assert the **absence** of the request rather than a 403? A 403 in the network log means the guard is not doing its job.

No treasury or fiscal reviewer: this wave touches neither module.

## Deploy

Spec §8's wave-0b runbook, with Q-0b-4's two invocations. **`tenants:seed RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` is not in this sequence at all** — on a marked tenant with `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE=false` it takes the preservation branch and writes nothing, so the 0b keys would never reach a tenant whose routes are already gated on them: 403 for everyone including `admin`.

### The two invocations are ONE shell wrapper, and the wrapper is what writes the status file (plan gate r1 **BLOCKER B0b-6**)

Rev 1 listed the two commands and then a step *"write the status file, on both arms… `=ok` on success"* — with no executable definition of *success*. A first invocation that **fails** followed by a second that **succeeds** would have written `permissions_ensure=ok` over a fleet that never received twelve of the nineteen keys. Rev 1's Q-0b-1 also claimed `permissions:ensure-fleet` *writes* the status file; **it does not** — the command renders per-tenant and aggregate markers and returns an exit code, and nothing in `EnsurePermissionsFleet::handle()` touches a file. **Rev 2 corrects the claim and makes success atomic.**

Create `scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh` (**a wave-0b deliverable, committed in Task 14, deleted by wave 1 with the commands it wraps**):

~~~bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Wave 0b permission deploy. TWO invocations, ONE success condition.
#
# The status file is written HERE, not by the command: `permissions:ensure-fleet`
# renders markers and returns an exit code, and no single exit code can express
# "both halves of a two-invocation deploy succeeded". Writing it from inside
# either invocation would let a failed first run be overwritten by a successful
# second one — a fleet missing twelve of the nineteen keys, recorded as healthy.
#
# Deleted in wave 1, in the same commit that deletes permissions:ensure(-fleet).
set -euo pipefail

STATUS_FILE="${PERMISSIONS_ENSURE_STATUS_FILE:-/var/run/autoerp/permissions-ensure.status}"
ARTISAN="${ARTISAN:-php artisan}"

mkdir -p "$(dirname "$STATUS_FILE")"

# `>` truncates, so a recovered deploy clears a previous failure rather than
# appending to it.
fail() {
  printf '%s\n' "permissions_ensure=failed:$1" > "$STATUS_FILE"
  echo "PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-DEPLOY FAILED at $1 — status written to $STATUS_FILE" >&2
  exit 1
}

# Invocation 1 — the twelve admin-only keys. No --grant-to: a flat --grant-to
# list would give a manager twelve keys the spec makes admin-only.
$ARTISAN permissions:ensure-fleet \
  --keys=channels.view --keys=channels.create --keys=channels.update --keys=channels.operate \
  --keys=categories.view --keys=categories.create --keys=categories.update --keys=categories.delete \
  --keys=progression.view --keys=progression.manage \
  --keys=purchase-hub.orders.create --keys=companies.create \
  || fail "invocation_1_admin_only_keys"

# Invocation 2 — the seven template keys, all in ONE run, because the
# eligibility snapshot is taken once per role before any of that role's
# additions are written.
$ARTISAN permissions:ensure-fleet \
  --keys=credit-notes.cancel \
  --keys=services.view --keys=services.create --keys=services.update \
  --keys=service-categories.view --keys=service-categories.create --keys=service-categories.update \
  --grant-to=manager --grant-to=general_manager \
  || fail "invocation_2_template_keys"

printf '%s\n' "permissions_ensure=ok" > "$STATUS_FILE"
echo "PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-DEPLOY OK — both invocations exited 0; status written to $STATUS_FILE"
~~~

`set -euo pipefail` plus the explicit `|| fail` on each invocation means **`ok` is written only when both exited 0**, and any other outcome leaves `failed:<which>` naming the half that broke. Add `--dry-run` to both invocations via `ARTISAN` or a `--dry-run` pass-through when rehearsing (Task 14 Step 6 runs them directly, not through the wrapper, because a dry run must not write a status file at all).

| # | Step | Notes |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | `bash scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh` | **one command.** It runs both `permissions:ensure-fleet` invocations under `set -euo pipefail`, and writes `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-ensure.status` — `permissions_ensure=ok` **only if both exited 0**, otherwise `permissions_ensure=failed:<which>`. Additive, marker-safe, idempotent. |
| 2 | `php artisan permission:cache-reset` | already wired at boot (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:190-191`), run explicitly here because the deploy wrote grants out of band. |
| 3 | `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map` | the FE map's input grew by nineteen keys. |

**Health check (the wave-0b deploy-checklist row).** *After the 0b deploy, confirm `scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh` exited **zero** and that `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-ensure.status` reads `permissions_ensure=ok`. A `permissions_ensure=failed:<which>` names which of the two invocations broke. If not, grep the container log for the `PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-FLEET` aggregate line — it names every failed or blocked tenant id with its reason — resolve the cause (a `tenant_not_ready` tenant needs its rolling migration to succeed first) and re-run `php artisan permissions:ensure-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug> --keys=… --grant-to=…` for those tenants, **each selector resolving to exactly one tenant** per §4.3.3's step-1a contract, before considering the deploy complete.*

**Verification.** The per-tenant `PERMISSIONS-ENSURE` marker shows non-zero `created`/`granted` on the first run and `created=0 granted=0` on a re-run (EC-32c). Every tenant named in `admin_only=` goes to residual **R-0b-7**.

**Rollback point.** Revert the wave's commits. The added `permissions` rows are **inert** once no route references them; `permissions:prune-orphans` is **not** run as part of a rollback (it does not exist until wave 3, and it is a deliberate operator action even then).

---

## Rollback

Every task is one self-contained, path-scoped commit. They are independent in the reverse direction, with three ordering constraints:

| Revert | Effect | Must also revert |
|---|---|---|
| Task 14 (handback) | documentation only | — |
| Task 13 (convention-09 tests) | three tests disappear | — |
| Task 12 (CI) | the ratchet goes dark in CI again and anti-growth returns to skipped | — |
| Task 11 (glossary) | six rows disappear; the lane's General-manager row is untouched either way | — |
| Task 10 (`PermissionSeeder`) | the colliding seeder and its seven test references return | — |
| Task 9 (`RequireAnyPermission`) | the 403 body loses `details`; T2's generic message survives | — |
| Task 8 (counting-item gate) | that write becomes ungated | its baseline entry and ceiling revert in the same commit |
| Task 7 (batch gates) | the two batch writes fall back to the flag-conditional middleware, i.e. **ungated** by default | same |
| Task 6 (eighteen keys + gates) | forty-one routes become ungated; the keys leave the catalogue | Task 13 first (its tests use the keys). **The already-deployed `permissions` rows survive on tenants and are inert.** |
| Task 5 (`credit-notes.cancel`) | the key leaves the catalogue and that route 403s everyone again | Task 6 first |
| Task 4 (ensure + wiring) | the two commands disappear and the eight writers lose the lock | Tasks 5–8 remain valid but **undeployable** — nothing can reach a marked tenant. Revert 6 and 5 too, or the routes are gated on keys no tenant can receive. |
| Task 3 (`TenantFleetRunner`) | the fleet iterator disappears | Task 4 first |
| Task 2 (`PermissionWriteLock`) | the lock helper disappears | Tasks 4 and 3 first |
| Task 1 (baseline + rename map) | the oracles disappear | Task 4 first |

**Two migrations? No — this wave adds none, changes no column and creates no table.** A full revert of the lane returns the tree to its pre-wave state; what it cannot revert is the `permissions` rows and `role_has_permissions` pivots a deploy already wrote. Those are **additive and inert** once no route references them, which is exactly why the deploy is safe to run before the code is certain.

### Findings NOT applied, with the reason

| Gate ID | Why not |
|---|---|
| **M0b-2**, the ordering half | Applied as far as it goes (every red is now named and reproducible), but **not** by re-sequencing Tasks 1–4 so that every test is authored before its implementation. Doing so would mean interleaving the supplied class bodies with their test bodies at a per-method granularity, which makes the plan unreadable as a dispatch document and does not change what an executing agent must produce. The critique is recorded in the handback as an execution-discipline note instead: **write each test file before the implementation it covers**, and if a red can only be produced by removing a branch, say so in the evidence. |

### Signature changes for wave 1

**None.** Every signature wave 1 consumes is unchanged by this round:

| Contract | Status after rev 2 |
|---|---|
| `PermissionWriteLock::acquire(string): void` / `::key(string): string` | **unchanged** |
| `LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants()` / `::wave0bAdditions(string)` / `::version0(string)` / `::matchesPreWave0b(string, array)` / `::matchesVersion0(string, array)` | **unchanged**. `WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS` gained an `admin` **row**; the constant's shape (`array<string, list<string>>`) and every method signature are identical. Wave 1's adoption arithmetic gets *more correct*, not different — a pristine admin now adopts as pristine, which is what wave 1 already expects |
| `PermissionRenameMap::entries()` / `::sources()` / `::targets()` / `::to(string)` / `::canonicalise(list)` | **unchanged** |
| `TenantFleetRunner::run(array, string, Closure): FleetRunResult`, `FleetTenantOutcome`, `FleetOutcome` | **unchanged** |
| `TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest::fleetCommands()` | **now exists**, which is what wave 1 needs — its plan promises to "extend" a provider rev 1 never created. Wave 1 appends one row: `'permissions:sync-fleet' => ['permissions:sync-fleet', ['--dry-run' => true], 'PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET']` |

**Two additions wave 1 should consume rather than re-invent**, neither of which changes an existing signature:

1. **`EnsurePermissionsResult::markerLine(string $tenantId, bool $write)`** gained its second parameter. This class is **deleted by wave 1** in the same commit that lands `permissions:sync`, so the change reaches wave 1 only as a shape to copy: the sync marker should render `mode=` from its own DTO for the same reason.
2. **`Tests\Architecture\Support\PermissionWriterCensus`** is new and is the class wave 1's M1-3 needs. Wave 1 adds `PermissionSyncService` and `TemplateDeltaApplier` to `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` — and `TemplateDeltaApplier`, which explicitly does **not** take the lock and relies on its callers, belongs in **`LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`** with `PermissionSyncService`'s locking method as its entry point, not in `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`. Under rev 1's file-substring test it would have passed on its own disclaiming docblock; under rev 2's it must prove the caller relationship. **Wave 1's plan must re-pin to this class name and this three-way split.**
3. **`scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh`** joins the list of things wave 1 deletes in the commit that lands `permissions:sync` — alongside `EnsurePermissions`, `EnsurePermissionsFleet`, `EnsurePermissionsRunner`, `DryRunRollback`, **`AdminRoleMissing`**, `EnsurePermissionsResult` and `EnsurePermissionsOutcome`. Residual R-0b-2 is updated to say so.
